# Pakistan — English books

**PAKISTAN OPENED 2026-11-03 under the user's all-closed-countries ruling.** Pakistan is CLOSED in the library. Its recorded English set is 13 books — 13 student's + 0 teacher, Nursery (Pre-I) and Grades 1–9, 11 and 12 — 13 in hand, 0 link-only. **Every one of the 13 is a Punjab book**: the Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board (PCTB), Lahore, carries Nursery through Grade 11, and the Grade 12 book is issued under PCTB's successor authority as printed on its own cover, PECTAA (Punjab Education, Curriculum, Training and Assessment Authority). No other provincial board stands in the counted set — the library's Sindh-board English material exists only as banked links in the download list and is not counted here. There is **no Grade 10 English book in the counted record** (one is held outside it — see the extras below), and **no teacher's book of any grade exists in Pakistan's record at all**.

The set was verified in both directions before reading. The queue row and the closed-52 inventory agree on 13; the push-state record confirms exactly the 13 paths (`nursery`, `grade-01`…`grade-09`, `grade-11`, `grade-11/english-advance`, `grade-12`, all `gov/pakistan/english/student/full-year.pdf`). textbooks.tsv holds 596 Pakistan rows, heavily duplicated from repeated Telegram sourcing; its English rows resolve to the same 13 plus the extras below. **Three further English files are held in the library but NOT counted, and were not read as part of this set** (identities cover-verified; the parent decides whether they are ever read or counted): (1) an **older-generation English 9** (plain PCTB cover, skills-grid contents; 148 pp, repo tree at `grade-09/gov/pakistan/english/full-year.pdf`) — a different, older book from the counted 2023-curriculum Grade 9; (2) **English Grammar & Composition 9-10, 2014-15**, Punjab Textbook Board (196 pp, held at `grade-09/gov/pakistan/english/grammar-composition-full-year.pdf`) — a grammar/composition book, not a grade textbook row; (3) **English X** (172 pp, repo tree at `grade-10/gov/pakistan/english/full-year.pdf`) — an older-style scanned Grade 10 book, the only Grade 10 English held anywhere. Grabs: Grades 3 and 5 came from the repo tree through the authenticated API route; the other 11 counted books are Telegram-secured in the library and were pulled from the @liqlearns_textbooks group; staging copies of Nursery, Grades 1, 2 and 9 on disk MD5-match the Telegram downloads exactly. **The true count is the recorded 13: 13 files, 13 distinct books, all MD5s distinct, no duplicates, no grade swaps — 1,694 PDF pages in total.** Every cover and title page was verified against its record row (filenames did not lie in this set); the record corrections the covers proved are listed at the end of this header.

**Citation check: 294 anchors filed across the 13 sections** (Nursery–Grade 5 band 132; Grades 6–8 band 74; Grades 9–12 band 88), each band checked by an independent checker who had not read the books, with failed anchors corrected in place — six corrections carry the in-text mark "(anchor corrected at the independent citation check, 2026-11-04)" and the checkers' further body corrections are incorporated in the sections (Grade 8's p.54 example-list wording, the Nursery "My Self" page, Grade 4's exact exercise wording among them). The Grades 6–8 check verified 74 of 74 anchors on rendered pages. The coordinator additionally spot-verified anchors on the real PDFs for every band (Nursery pp.1–2; Grade 2 and Grade 4 contents pages; Grade 6 p.6; Grade 7 p.130; Grade 8 p.6; Grade 9's final pages; Grade 11 Book I p.2; Grade 12's back matter). **One disclosure belongs on the record:** during this wave a filesystem fault split the workers' copies, and divergent duplicate scans of several grades circulated among them (a Grade 9 scan carrying board model papers, a Grade 12 scan carrying marking schemes, a 12-unit Grade 3 scan, a unit-based Grade 5 scan, an ILMI-reprint Book III). Where a worker draft described a different printing, the section in this file follows **the library's secured file** — the staging/GitHub copy, MD5-verified — and the coordinator re-verified those sections against it directly; the Grade 11 Book III section was read by the coordinator from the secured file outright, because the band draft described the other printing. The alternate scans exist and are noted in the relevant sections, but nothing in this file is cited from them.

**Series verdicts (what the 13 books, read whole, actually do).** The set is three generations wearing one uniform. (i) **Primary, SNC 2020** (Nursery–Grade 5): a complete phonics-and-skills ladder — letters, sounds, blends, digraphs, sight words, then grammar in "Read Me" boxes — with one break in the pattern: the held Grade 5 book is the board's **older-format reader** (13 texts, no SNC line, no Learning Outcomes boxes), not the SNC book its row suggests. (ii) **Middle, SNC 2022** (Grades 6–8): the grammar-heavy years — Grade 6 teaches relative clauses and a full reported-speech course (Units 11–12), Grade 7 adds an IPA pronunciation chart, Grade 8 is a PCTB + Oxford University Press co-production with a dictionary appendix and an errata page. (iii) **The 2023-curriculum pair** (Grade 9 on the Updated/Revised National Curriculum 2023, Experimental Edition; Grade 12 on the Revised National Curriculum 2023 under PECTAA). Beside them stand the **pre-SNC intermediate literature books** (English Book I — 15 short stories — and English Book III — 3 plays and 20 poems — both April 2018 printings on a 2003 NOC basis), which teach no language system at all. **Honest negatives for the whole set: the passive voice is never taught as a topic in any of the 13 books** (Grade 6's cover word-cloud even advertises "Active Passive" that no unit delivers; Grade 9 names it once inside a participles note); **no conditional type is ever taught** (if-sentences occur as reading input only); **the present perfect continuous IS taught in Grade 8** (Unit 5, printed pp.47–56: the "Present Perfect Continuous Tense" heading on p.54, a full for & since spread, and a perfect-or-perfect-continuous exercise); elsewhere it is not taught — Grade 7 carries one passing mention only, and Grade 6 names the forms only inside reported-speech conversion charts. [Corrected 2026-11-03: this verdict originally read "never taught (one passing mention in Grade 7)," generalizing Grade 7's situation to the whole set and missing Grade 8 — the book sections below disprove it, and the Pakistan merge caught it.]; reported speech is taught properly only in Grades 6 and 9; **examination preparation exists only in Grade 12** (a pairing scheme and one model paper; the model paper is printed without answers or a marking scheme for its answers) — the secured Grade 9 copy has none; **no book prints an ISBN**; and answer keys are absent almost everywhere (Grade 12 prints exactly two small keys). Copy state: the held **Grade 8 file is incomplete** — its contents promise a Review-03 on printed p.123 that the PDF does not contain (the file ends at printed p.122); every other counted file is complete and in order.

**Cover-proved record corrections.** (1) Grade 11's record title ("English Class 11 SNC") is wrong for the held book: it is **English Book I (Short Stories), session 2018-19**, pre-SNC. (2) The Grade 11 advance row is **English Book III (Plays and Poems)**, PCTB, April 2018, 20th impression, published by Malik Muhammad Afzal & Sons, Lahore — not a separate "advance course". (3) Grade 12's authority as printed is **PECTAA**, not PCTB. (4) Grade 9's basis is the **Updated/Revised National Curriculum of Pakistan 2023**, and its imprint's printing table (date, copies, price) is blank as printed. (5) Grades 6–8 rest on **SNC 2022**, Nursery–Grade 4 on **SNC 2020**, and Grade 5 prints **no curriculum basis at all**. (6) Pakistan's record holds **no Grade 10 English row and no teacher's books** — both gaps are the record's, not this read's.

**Still owed by the library side:** a complete Grade 8 file (with Review-03, printed p.123); a read/count decision on the three held-but-uncounted extras — above all **English X**, the only Grade 10 English in hand, since the counted record has no Grade 10 at all; and any teacher's books for Pakistan, none of which exist in the record. The three extras were identity-checked only and are not part of the 13 read here.


## Pakistan — English, Pre-I (Nursery) Primer (file: nursery.pdf)

# Pakistan — Pre-I English Primer (Nursery) — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** Cover reads "PRE-I / ENGLISH / PRIMER". Title page (PDF p.2) reads "English Primer".
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore" (cover, title page and back cover).
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" and "One Nation, One Curriculum" (cover and title page).
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No year, edition statement or ISBN is printed anywhere in the front matter checked (cover, title page, credits page PDF p.3). The credits page names a long list of authors/reviewers and a "Director Manuscripts" but prints no date or ISBN.
- **PDF page count:** 150 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 7. Verified on foliated pages: PDF p.8 carries printed "1" (the "Greetings" opener page), PDF p.9 = printed 2 ("My Self"), PDF p.149 = printed 142 (last content page). (Coordinator's correction at assembly, 2026-11-04: an earlier wording of this line named printed p.1 as "My Self"; the rendered pages show printed p.1 is "Greetings" and "My Self" opens on printed p.2 — which is also where the independent citation check placed it.) Front matter PDF pp.1–7 is unfoliated (cover, title page, credits, two contents spreads PDF pp.4–7). PDF p.150 is the back cover (national anthem in Urdu, board imprint), unfoliated.
- **Contents:** The printed contents (PDF pp.4–7) lists 82 numbered items, from "1 My Self — 1" to "82 Creative Writing — 142".

## 2. Architecture

A combined textbook-cum-workbook for pre-primary (ECE/Nursery). Every page carries a coloured teacher-guidance strip at the foot (instructions beginning e.g. "Introduce letter…", "Trace the letters…"), so teaching notes are built into the pupil book; there is no separate answer key.

Three repeating blocks:

1. **Theme / rhyme pages** (single pages, picture-led, with a short rhyme or labelled pictures): self, home, family, school, country, daily routine, weather, seasons, body, courtesy, festivals, hygiene, food, living/non-living, nature, professions, citizenship, position words.
2. **Letter units Aa–Zz** — the spine of the book. Each letter takes exactly 3 printed pages: (i) introduction page — big letter pair, a two-line phonic chant, 2–4 pictured key words and a sight-word list in the teacher strip; (ii) "Trace and Colour" — dotted letter forms with stroke arrows plus 4 outline pictures to colour; (iii) "Trace and Write" — pattern-tracing lines, a "Circle" letter-recognition cluster, and four-line (blue/pink) handwriting lines. After every 6 letters a one-page "Reinforcement Activity" (match capital to small letter and draw) closes the group: A–F, G–L, M–R, S–Z.
3. **Word-building and writing tail** (printed pp.104–142): whole-alphabet Trace and Write practice (capitals then smalls), Sky/Grass/Root letters, Vowels and Consonants, five CVC word-family pages, missing-vowel and beginning-sound worksheets, a/an, Phrases, full stop + capital letter, adding "s", a Sight Words wall page, "this is" / "these are" sentence frames, three digraph pages (th, ch, sh), -ing Action Words, in/out, "ee" and "oo" word lists, then Character Sketch + Creative Writing pages (My Self, My Mother, My Toy, My Friend, and a story starter).

## 3. Unit-by-unit (printed page numbers)

**Opening themes**
- Printed p.2 My Self — draw "I look like / Things I like"; talk about self, hygiene, posture. (Printed p.1 is the "Greetings" opener — see the coordinator's note in §1.)
- p.2 My Self (cont.) — "Draw and Colour"; self-portrait.
- p.3 My Home — labelled rooms: bedroom, kitchen, living room, washroom; role play.
- p.4 My Family — family rhyme; labels grandfather, grandmother, father, mother, brother, sister; family tree at home.
- p.5 My School — school scene; labels library, classroom.
- p.6 My Country — Pakistan rhyme beside Minar-e-Pakistan; teacher strip names the four provinces, Quaid-e-Azam, Allama Iqbal, Urdu, shalwar kameez; make a flag.
- p.7 My Day — daily-routine rhyme (wake up, pray, brush teeth, breakfast, school, study, play, sleep); sequence flashcards.
- p.8 Weather — Cloudy, Windy, Rainy, Sunny; weather wheel.
- pp.9–10 Seasons — Spring, Summer (p.9); Autumn, "Winters" (p.10), each 2–3 simple sentences.
- p.11 Hot and Cold — heater, ice-cream, iron, ice-cube.
- p.18 Parts of the Body — labelled figure: hair, head, eye, ear, nose, neck, lips, arm, hand, finger, "Knee", leg, toe, foot; body-parts song; good/bad touch note in teacher strip.
- p.22 Courtesy Words — thank you, sorry, excuse me, please; role play.
- p.26 Days of the Week — days song naming Monday–Sunday; caterpillar craft.
- p.33 Festivals — festival rhyme; teacher strip covers Eid-ul-Fitr, Eid-ul-Azha, Christmas, Diwali, Nauroz, Baisakhi and respecting other religions; greeting card.
- p.41 Handwashing — 10 pictured handwashing steps beside a handwashing rhyme; Global Handwashing Day (15 October) in teacher strip.
- p.48 Junk and Healthy Food — sort six pictured foods, write "H"/"J".
- p.55 Living and Non-living Things — draw two of each.
- p.63 Natural and Man-made Resources — two-column picture sort.
- p.70 Professions — match people (teacher, pilot, chef, nurse/doctor in strip) to workplace pictures.
- p.71 Plant Life Cycle — "Life Cycle of a Bean Plant", 6 numbered stages; plant care.
- p.78 Responsible Citizen — plant trees, save trees, throw litter in bin, save water.

## 4. Grammar / vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics:** Full alphabet A–Z in order, one letter-sound at a time, each with a spoken chant, initial-sound picture words and sight-word lists; then consolidation in strict sequence: letter formation (sky/grass/root placement on four lines) → vowels vs consonants → single-onset CVC blending in five families (-an, -et, -in, -ot, -un) → missing-vowel and beginning-sound completion → initial digraphs th/ch/sh (initial position only) → vowel digraphs ee/oo as whole-word lists. No long/short vowel terminology, no blending beyond CVC and the listed digraph words, no final digraphs or blends (no -sh/-ch endings, no consonant clusters).
- **Vocabulary:** ~8–10 sight words per letter in teacher strips (≈200 letter-linked words) + 4 pictured words per introduction page + theme sets (rooms, family, body, weather, seasons, days, courtesy phrases, professions, foods). Pakistani/local lexis throughout: names Babar, Basit, Catherine, Gulnar, Kashif; shalwar kameez, Minar-e-Pakistan, mango as national fruit.
- **Grammar (all implicit, worksheet-level):** a/an choice by initial sound (one page); plural by adding -s (one page, plus reused in "these are"); sentence frame "This is a …" vs "These are …"; capital letter + full stop as the only punctuation taught; phrase vs sentence defined only in teacher strips; -ing added to five action stems. No tense system, no verb paradigms, no pronouns set, no questions beyond the modelled "What is your name?"
- **Skills:** Listening/speaking dominate the first half (rhymes sung aloud, picture talk, role play on nearly every theme page); reading is word-recognition only until the CVC tail; writing progresses trace → copy on four lines → fill gaps → 3 short creative-writing topics with a planning web for two of them.

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

Checked systematically across all 142 printed pages:

- **Phonics gaps:** No phoneme chart/IPA; sounds are taught only via chants and initial-sound words. No final-sound work except rime families; no consonant blends (bl-, st-, -nd etc.); no silent letters; no long-vowel patterns beyond ee/oo word lists; digraphs only th/ch/sh and only word-initial.
- **Alphabet:** Complete A–Z coverage — nothing missing; but letter *names* vs *sounds* are never distinguished explicitly.
- **Tenses:** None. No present/past/future forms are named or drilled; verbs appear only as -ing stems (p.132) and in rhymes.
- **Passive voice:** Absent. **Conditionals:** Absent. **Reported speech:** Absent.
- **Other grammar absent:** No articles beyond the single a/an page (no "the" lesson); no prepositions except in/out (p.133) and phrases "on/in" inside examples; no adjectives, comparatives, possessives, plurals beyond -s (no -es, no irregular plurals); no question formation beyond one modelled exchange; no negation.
- **Exam preparation:** None — no test papers, no marks scheme, no revision section.
- **Answer keys:** None. Teacher guidance is activity instruction in page-foot strips, not answers.
- **Also absent:** No glossary, no word list index, no Urdu translations of vocabulary, no numbers/colours/shapes units in English (colours and shapes appear only incidentally), no listening audio reference, no handwriting joined/cursive forms (print script only).

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Printed p.10: heading "Winters" (plural) over text beginning "Winter is cold."
- Printed p.112: the small-letter practice page heads its rows with capital tiles "V", "W", "X", "Z" alongside small "u", "y" — inconsistent with the page's own "small letters" sequence (a–f, g–m, n–t pages use small tiles throughout).
- Printed p.121: heading printed "Begining Sounds" (one 'n').
- Printed p.126 (Sight Words): the list contains a stray single letter "d" as an entry; "yes" appears twice; "hut" appears as the final sight word where a high-frequency word is expected.
- Printed p.129–131 headings vary in form: "Introduction of Digraphs" (th), "Introduction of Digraphs 'ch' words", "Introduction of 'sh' words Digraphs" — inconsistent heading wording across the three digraph pages.
- Printed p.132: action stems printed as "swimm", "writ", "jogg" for the child to complete with "ing" — the doubling is presented without any rule or comment.
- Teacher-strip typos seen: p.63 "Explain to childern" (for children); p.70 "every (profession is respectable nurse, teacher, pilot, doctor, etc.)" — garbled parenthesis; p.104 "draw pictures infront of the matched" (sentence trails off).
- Printed p.18: "Knee" is capitalised mid-list while all other body labels are lower case.

## 7. Copy defects of this PDF file

- **Watermark:** Every content page carries the diagonal grey watermark "Web version of PCTB Textbook / Not For Sale" across the page centre. It is part of the official web release, not damage, but it overlays text/pictures throughout.
- **Printed p.29 (PDF p.36), Ee "Trace and Write":** a large unrelated full-colour illustration (a green vehicle/bench scene) is printed across the middle of the handwriting-lines area, obscuring part of the Ee practice rows — a layout/imposition error on this page; the equivalent page for every other letter is clean.
- **Printed p.87 (PDF p.94), Uu "Trace and Colour":** the "up" picture box contains a solid black rectangle block beside the figure — appears as a blacked-out/failed image fill.
- **Folio continuity:** Checked across the whole file — printed folios run 1–142 with no missing, duplicated or out-of-order pages; PDF pp.1–7 front matter and p.150 back cover are unfoliated as expected. No blank pages, no truncated pages, no misbound spreads (single-page-per-PDF-page throughout; no cover spreads in this file).
- No stamps, handwriting or scan skew found; pages are clean digital renders, not scans.

## Pakistan — English, Grade 1 (file: g01.pdf)

# Pakistan — English Grade 1 — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** Cover and title page read "ENGLISH / Grade 1" (title page: "English / Grade 1").
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "PUNJAB CURRICULUM AND TEXTBOOK BOARD, LAHORE" (title page); spine and cover: "Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore".
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" and "One Nation, One Curriculum" (cover/title page). Credits page (PDF p.5): "This book is based on Single National Curriculum 2020 and has been approved by the National Review Committee."
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No publication year or ISBN is printed. The credits page carries a red stamp "Experimental Edition" and blank ruled lines headed "Edition / Publication Date / Quantity" with nothing filled in. Author named: Maryam Zafar; Editors: Haroon Rashid, Mahmood Ahmad; Supervision: Muhammad Rafique Tahir, Joint Educational Advisor, National Curriculum Council.
- **PDF page count:** 118 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 6. Verified: PDF p.7 = printed 1 (Unit 1 opener), PDF p.117 = printed 111 (last content page). PDF p.1 is a front+back cover spread; PDF pp.2–6 are title page, two contents spreads, credits, "About the Book"; PDF p.118 repeats the cover spread (see §7).

## 2. Architecture

Eleven numbered units plus four Review units (Review 1–4), mapped in a two-spread contents grid (PDF pp.3–4) whose columns are: Unit / Page / Themes / Sub-themes / Oral Communication / Reading and Critical Thinking / Language Focus / Grammar / Writing — i.e. the contents itself is the syllabus map.

Every teaching unit follows the same skeleton:
- **Unit opener** — title, a boxed "Learning Outcomes" list, full-page art.
- **Getting Started** — picture talk + "Let's Talk" oral questions.
- **Reading text** — a short story, poem or procedure, framed by Pre-reading / While-reading / Post-reading prompts.
- **A) Oral Communication** — 1. Learning the Sounds (phonics focus), 2. Learning to Speak (a short modelled dialogue to practise in pairs).
- **B) Reading and Critical Thinking** — Reading Comprehension: answer questions, fill blanks (word bank or MCQ), choose correct option.
- **C) Language Focus** — 1. Vocabulary Building (3–4 words with one-line English meanings; themed word sets), 2. Learning to Spell (sight words fitted into shape boxes; circle-the-correct-spelling; missing letters), 3. Grammar (one or two points per unit, each with a "Read Me" definition box, examples, then exercises).
- **D) Writing** — 1. Learning to Write (copying, listing, labelling), 2. Creative Writing (short guided production).
- A "Teaching Point" strip at the foot of most pages gives the teacher script-level guidance. Review units (pp.27–29, 50–52, 90–92, 110–111) recycle the same A–D sections without a new reading text.

## 3. Unit-by-unit (printed page numbers; unit start page from the contents grid, verified on unit openers)

- **Unit 1 — Time to Recall (printed p.1).** A pure foundations unit, no story. Getting Started: look-and-say pictured words, initial sounds (p.2). Alphabet in series and random order, letter sounds (p.3); pre-writing strokes — horizontal, vertical, slanted, zigzag, curves, circles (p.4); trace-and-colour train picture (p.5); trace/copy all capital+small pairs Aa–Zz on four lines (pp.5–6); write capitals in order; before/after letters (p.6); Dictation of letters; trace/copy three-letter words sun, ant, pan, rat, jug, hen (p.7). Contents grid: Language Focus "Three-letter words"; Writing "Pre-writing, The alphabet, Three-letter words"; no grammar.
- **Unit 2 — My Family and I (p.8).** Reading: first-person text by Sa'ad, six years old — family members, school day, lunch, homework, play, bedtime story (p.10). Sounds: initial sounds; segmenting fan/box/cup/net/pin/cat letter by letter (p.11). Dialogue: greeting/introduction "Assalaamu Alaikum! I am Mariam…" (p.11). Comprehension: questions, word-bank blanks (green/orange/like), MCQs (pp.12–13). Vocabulary: study, complete, toy + Number Words one–ten pictured (p.13). Spelling: sight words to, and, he, you, I, the; one- vs two-syllable words explained in a Read Me (p.14); circle correct spelling (play/clock/train/story/brother rows, p.15). Grammar: **Nouns (Naming Words)** defined and matched to pictures (p.15); **Pronouns (Substitution Words)** I/you/we/he/she/it/they with picture sentences and fill-ins (p.16). Writing: copy one-syllable words; find five one-syllable words in a mini-paragraph; Creative Writing = interview a friend (name/age/class/parents/favourite toy form) (p.17).
- **Unit 3 — Cobbler, Cobbler (p.18).** Theme: professions. Getting Started: farmer/doctor/policeman/teacher pictures (p.19). Poem "Cobbler, Cobbler, mend my shoe…" with pre/while/post-reading (p.20). Sounds: **consonant blends cl, bl, dr, br** with word tables and gap-fill (p.21). Dialogue: greetings + polite request for crayons (p.21). Comprehension + naming vs action words tables (p.22); action-word picture matching; poem cloze from word bank eight/down/crown/shoe/two (p.23). Vocabulary: cobbler, mend, stitch, crown; **Rhyming Words** shoe/two, pen/hen, down/crown (p.24). Spelling: sight words it, of, in, was, said, with. Grammar: nouns fill-in; pronouns — rearrange jumbled sentences and circle the pronoun, with capital letter + full stop reminded (p.25). Writing: trace/copy four sentences; Creative Writing: complete profession definitions from word bank cobbler/teacher/doctor/gardener (p.26).
- **Review 1 (p.27).** Underline blends (brick, block, drop…); circle initial letter sounds; role-play self/family sentences; comprehension questions on Units 2–3; underline nouns / circle action words; write meanings of cobbler/crown/stitch; name pictures; pronoun tick-and-fill; write three sentences on what you want to be; three one-syllable words (pp.27–29).
- **Unit 4 — Let's Have Fun! (p.30).** Reading: procedure text — Huma and Hina make pencil-shaving flowers: things needed + 4-step Method (pp.32–33). Sounds: **initial digraphs ch, ph, sh** (child/phone/shop tables) (p.33); blends revisited in comprehension (p.34). Speaking: polite commands dialogues ("kindly go and wash your hands") (pp.33–34). Comprehension MCQs (p.35). Vocabulary: collect, draw, paste, sharpen + a greetings/courtesy dialogue (p.35). Spelling: sight words had, but, they, that, for, on, she, his; circle correct spelling (p.36). Grammar: **Singular and Plural Nouns** — add -s (pencil/pencils…), write plurals (p.36–37); **'This' and 'That'** pointing words (p.37); **Articles 'a' or 'an'** by initial sound, with practice (p.37). Writing: naming+action words from pictures; five sentences on daily routine; Creative Writing: sequence the lemonade-making steps (pp.38–39).
- **Unit 5 — Sharing is Caring (p.40).** Poem: "Let Others Share" by Edward Anthony (p.42). Sounds: initial sounds — change the first letter to make new words (run/gun/fun…); **digraphs ch, sh, ph, th in initial AND final positions** (chips/much, ship/fish, photo/graph, thick/bath) (pp.43, 45). Colours (red, green, blue, yellow, brown) and Shapes (square, circle, triangle, rectangle, oval, star) (p.45). Speaking: expressing needs dialogue (asking father for snacks) (p.43). Vocabulary: insist, certain, brat; rhyming words son/fun, rain/train, that/brat (p.44). Spelling: sight words is, up, all, look, her, there, be, some (p.46). Grammar: **Masculine and Feminine** nouns (boy/girl, bull/cow, man/woman, horse/mare; match father–mother etc.) (pp.46–47); **'These' and 'Those'** (p.47); articles a/an re-taught with pictured examples and labelling (pp.47–48). Writing: naming/action words from a park picture; Creative Writing: four sentences about "My Family" from a word bank (p.49).
- **Review 2 (p.50).** Circle digraphs (phone, shoes, tooth, cheese, graph); match same initial sounds; meanings of collect/brat/certain; plurals; a/an blanks; feminine forms; naming/action words from a picnic picture (pp.50–52).
- **Unit 6 — Blessings of Allah (p.53)** (title carries the Arabic honorific in brackets). Story: personified fruits and vegetables; Red Carrot is sad because Juicy Apple makes fun of her; moral — do not fight, be thankful (p.55). Sounds: initial and ending sounds (pp.55–56). Speaking: emotions — I am happy/sad/angry, draw faces (p.56). Comprehension: questions, three-letter words, rearrange-and-punctuate sentences, MCQs (pp.56–57). Vocabulary: healthy, sweet, rude; **Fruit and Vegetable Names** (apricot, watermelon, pineapple, strawberry / turnip, cucumber, okra, brinjal) with tick/circle sort (p.58). Spelling: sight words down, little, then, we, go, have, am, out (p.58). Grammar: **Proper Nouns** (capitalise particular person/place) (p.59); **Question Words** what/where/who (p.59); **Action Words** (verbs in -ing example sentences; underline) (p.60); **Omission of Articles** — plural nouns take no a/an (p.60). Writing: numbers 1–10 in words; four sentences on table manners; Creative Writing: complete "My Favourite Fruit" sentences (p.61).
- **Unit 7 — Classroom Manners (p.62).** Reading: dialogue-story — new girl Zara is welcomed; pupils recite nine classroom rules from a chart (keep classroom clean, respect teachers, queue, polite words…) (pp.64–65). Sounds: one-syllable rhyming pairs (class/glass, time/lime, run/fun, hat/bat) (p.66). Dialogue: congratulating on a spelling competition (p.66). Comprehension: questions; manners checklist to tick; read food labels (bread, milk, chips, ketchup) (p.67). Vocabulary: permission, queue, keep; **Common Objects** — classroom (chair, desk, board, school bag, pencil, book) vs home (sofa, TV, bed, lamp, iron, clock); **Left and Right** directions (pp.68–69). Spelling: sight words do, little, could, when, did, what, so, see (p.69). Grammar: **Wh Questions** why/when (p.70); **Use of 'Am', 'Is', 'Are'** (p.70) + tick-the-correct-option drill (p.71); **Adjectives of Size** tall/small/fat/thin (p.71). Writing: listing items under Clothes/Food/Classroom Objects; Creative Writing: sentences on keeping the classroom clean (p.72).
- **Unit 8 — Nature is Beautiful (p.73).** Getting Started: label four weathers (p.74). Poem: "It's Spring Time" by Winnifred Mott (p.75). Sounds: one-syllable words life, grew, nose, lack (p.76). Dialogue: taking turns to read in class (p.76). Comprehension + cloze (pp.76, 78). Vocabulary: glad, marbles, shout; rhyming find/kind, sunny/funny; **Parts of Body** hand, knee, eye, arm, nose, neck (p.78); alphabetical order; brainstorming/mind map "Myself" modelled, "My Teacher" to complete (p.77). Spelling: sight words work, try, today, fly, tell, much, keep, give (p.79). Grammar: **is/am/are** again (p.79); **Adjectives of Quality** nice/good/intelligent/neat/clean (p.80); **Capitalisation** — first letter of first word of a sentence; rewrite sentences capitalised (p.80). Writing: date/day/caption format on notebook page; fill missing information; Creative Writing: sentences on "Green Environment" (p.81).
- **Unit 9 — A Greeting Card (p.82).** Reading: three-step procedure — Ayyan and Maham make an Eid card (p.84). Sounds: sight words are, and, this, their (p.85). Speaking: requests dialogue — asking mother to buy card materials (p.85). Comprehension: questions; book **titles** — identify a book by its title, match Cinderella/The Ugly Duckling/The Lion and the Mouse/The Hare and the Tortoise covers to titles (p.86); MCQs. Vocabulary: half, share, greeting; **Ordinal Numbers** first–tenth (p.87). Spelling: sight words gave, open, has, only, hold. Grammar: **Adjectives of Colour** (p.88); **Full stop (.)** (p.88); **Requests** — polite request forms (p.88–89). Writing: make your own Eid card, write greetings, name + phone number on the back; Creative Writing: complete sentences about "my mother" (p.89).
- **Review 3 (p.90).** Match rhyming words; sight words in own sentences (that/are/on); comprehension on Units 7 and 9; alphabetical order; is/am/are choice; when/why questions; adjectives of size/quality/colour table; "My Best Friend" qualities mind map (pp.90–92).
- **Unit 10 — The Hare and the Tortoise (p.93).** Fable retold in simple past narrative (~200 words, p.95). Sounds: **/s/ and /z/ sounds in plurals** — full table (rabbits/cups/ducks vs beds/sparrows/rooms) with the voiced/unvoiced rule in the Teaching Point (p.96). Dialogue: Hare and Tortoise role-play (p.97). Comprehension: questions + word-bank cloze (hare/challenged/finish line/ashamed/rude — note "ashamed" is not in the printed story text, which says the hare "was very sad") (p.97). Vocabulary: challenge, cheer, proud, rude; **Animal Names** — wild (tiger, bear, elephant) vs farm (horse, hen, rabbit) (p.98). Spelling: sight words them, did, can, so, not + bank ten/soon/run/find/drink (pp.98–99). Grammar: **Words Showing Possession** — possessive adjectives my, your, our (p.99), then his/her/their follow in Unit 11; **Question Mark (?)** (p.100); **Commands** (p.100–101). Writing: fill speech bubbles with the given dialogue lines; Creative Writing: four sentences from a started paragraph about a parrot (p.101).
- **Unit 11 — Love Animals (p.102).** Poem: "My Kitty Cat" by Ryan Gibbs (p.104). Sounds: /s/ vs /z/ plurals again — make plurals and sort (p.105). Speaking: body-language signs (Victory / Well done / Perfect hand signs) (p.105). Comprehension: questions; **calendar** — read a 12-month calendar, months, ordinal position (p.106); underline capitals / circle punctuation (p.106). Vocabulary: dawn, adopt, stretch out; rhyming night/white, fed/head, mood/food (p.107). Spelling: sight words yes, will, me, would, my, this, one, like (p.108). Grammar: possessives **his, her, their** (p.108–109); **Comma** in a list (p.109). Writing: find rhyming pair in a short religious poem and add one; Creative Writing: five things about your pet (p.109).
- **Review 4 (p.110).** Plurals with /s/ vs /z/ sorting; discussion; comprehension on Units 10–11; rewrite with correct capitals and punctuation; vocabulary meanings (cheer/rude/dawn); his/her/their blanks; comma punctuation; circle the commands; write five sentences about a tortoise (pp.110–111).

## 4. Grammar / vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics:** Letter sounds + pre-writing (U1) → initial sounds and CVC segmenting (U2) → initial blends cl/bl/dr/br (U3) → initial digraphs ch/ph/sh (U4) → digraphs initial+final incl. th (U5) → initial/ending sounds (U6) → rhyming one-syllable words (U7) → syllable idea recycled (U8) → irregular sight words (U9) → plural pronunciation /s/ vs /z/ (U10–11). Rhyme work runs through almost every unit. IPA symbols appear only inside Learning Outcomes (/p/, /i/, /n/ example in U1–U2), not in pupil exercises.
- **Grammar sequence (contents grid verified against pages):** nouns + subject pronouns (U2) → nouns/pronouns consolidation (U3) → singular/plural -s, this/that, a/an (U4) → masculine/feminine, these/those, a/an (U5) → proper nouns, question words what/where/who, action words, article omission with plurals (U6) → wh-questions why/when, am/is/are, adjectives of size (U7) → am/is/are, adjectives of quality, capitalisation (U8) → adjectives of colour, full stop, requests (U9) → possessives my/your/our, question mark, commands (U10) → possessives his/her/their, comma (U11). Everything is present-tense be/have territory plus story past-tense input; no tense is ever named.
- **Vocabulary:** 3–4 glossed words per unit with one-line English definitions, plus themed sets (numbers 1–10, colours, shapes, fruits/vegetables, classroom/home objects, animals wild/farm, body parts, ordinals 1st–10th, months via calendar) and ~6–8 sight words per unit (≈70 across the book), recycled in every Review.
- **Writing:** trace/copy → one-syllable word copying → sentence copying with capital/full stop → listing/labelling → guided 3–5 sentence productions (interview form, My Family, daily routine, classroom cleanliness, Green Environment, My Mother, pet paragraph).

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

- **Tenses:** No tense is named or systematically taught — no present simple paradigm, no past tense lesson, no future forms. Past-tense verbs occur only inside the Unit 6/10 story texts as input.
- **Passive voice:** Absent. **Conditionals:** Absent. **Reported speech:** Absent (dialogues are practised as direct speech only; no reporting verbs lesson).
- **Also absent:** the article "the" as a taught point (a/an and omission only); prepositions as a set (in/on/at occur untaught); comparatives/superlatives; possessive 's (only possessive adjectives my/his/her…); object pronouns (me/him/her as objects not taught — subject pronouns only, plus possessive adjectives); question formation beyond wh-word prompts (no inversion/do-support); negation beyond incidental "do not"; irregular plurals (only -s plurals; no -es, no men/children type forms).
- **Phonics limits:** No long-vowel patterns (no magic-e, no vowel digraph lessons — ee/oo/ai etc. appear only inside words); digraphs limited to ch, sh, ph, th; blends limited to cl, bl, dr, br (plus review exposure); no final blends.
- **Exam preparation:** None — Reviews are workbook-style revision, not test papers; no marks, no model answers.
- **Answer keys:** None. Teaching Point strips guide the teacher but give no key.
- **Also absent:** No end-of-book glossary or word list; no Urdu meanings (all glosses are simple English); no days-of-week lesson (days appear only inside the calendar page, U11).

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Printed p.81: the model notebook caption is printed "Its Spring Time" — no apostrophe, in the very unit that teaches capitalisation; the poem's own title on p.75 is correctly "It's Spring Time".
- Printed p.84: "They give this card to their cousin Asma the before Eid day." — garbled word order (a word missing/misplaced).
- Printed p.57: MCQ items are numbered "• ii." mid-list — the second item under iv. is printed "ii. ___ was sitting quietly…" duplicating the exercise number.
- Printed p.97: cloze word bank includes "ashamed", but the story text on p.95 says the hare "was very sad" — bank and text do not match.
- Printed p.109: the three model comma sentences are typeset with words run together without spaces, e.g. "I love to eat apples,grapes,mangoes and bananas." appears with collapsed spacing ("Ilovetoeatapples…" in the printed setting).
- Printed p.10: text reads "I am a Pakistani." (article before nationality) — modelled as correct English for imitation.
- Printed p.35: "sharpen — to make something sharper;to become sharper" — missing space after the semicolon in the printed gloss.

## 7. Copy defects of this PDF file

- **Duplicated cover spread:** PDF p.1 and PDF p.118 are the same front+back cover spread (back cover with Urdu national anthem on the left, front cover on the right) — the spread appears at both the start and the end of the file.
- **Watermark:** Every page carries the diagonal "Web version of PCTB Textbook / Not for Sale" watermark (this is also the source of most of the file's sparse text layer — the extractable text is dominated by the watermark, which is why texts/g01.txt reads as repeated watermark fragments).
- **Folio continuity:** Printed folios run 1–111 continuously with no missing, duplicated or out-of-order content pages (checked unit by unit against the contents grid start pages: U1 p.1, U2 p.8, U3 p.18, Review 1 p.27, U4 p.30, U5 p.40, Review 2 p.50, U6 p.53, U7 p.62, U8 p.73, U9 p.82, Review 3 p.90, U10 p.93, U11 p.102, Review 4 p.110 — all match the pages as bound). No blank or truncated pages found.
- Text-layer note: g01 has a partial text layer, but page content was verified on rendered pages throughout, per the brief.

## Pakistan — English, Grade 2 (file: g02.pdf)

# Pakistan — English Grade 2 — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** Cover and title page read "ENGLISH / Grade 2" (title page: "English / Grade 2").
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "PUNJAB CURRICULUM AND TEXTBOOK BOARD, LAHORE" (title page). Credits page: "Publisher: Punjab Curriculum And Textbook Board Lahore. Printer: Fahad Publishing House Lahore."
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" and "One Nation, One Curriculum" (cover/title page). Credits: "This book is based on Single National Curriculum 2020 and has been approved by the National Review Committee."
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No ISBN printed. The credits page (PDF p.3) is dated: "Date of Printing — February 2022", with a print-run table (PMIU 533020, PEF 130025, PEIMA 46989, MLWC 141, Special Education 0, LNFBE 76165, Literacy (TSKL) 7390, Total Quantity 793730) and a red "Experimental Edition" stamp. Authors: Naheed Ahmed, Shazia Manzoor; Editors: Haroon Rashid, Mahmood Ahmad; Supervision: Muhammad Rafique Tahir.
- **PDF page count:** 126 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 6. Verified: PDF p.7 = printed 1 (Unit 1 opener), PDF p.125 = printed 119 (last content page). PDF p.1 is the front+back cover spread; PDF pp.2–6 are title page, credits, About the Book, two contents spreads; PDF p.126 repeats the cover spread (see §7).

## 2. Architecture

Same SNC series architecture as Grade 1, one step up: **12 units + 4 Reviews**, mapped in a two-spread contents grid (PDF pp.5–6) with the same columns (Themes / Sub-themes / Oral Communication / Reading and Critical Thinking / Language Focus / Grammar / Writing). Each unit: opener with Learning Outcomes → Getting Started + Let's Talk → reading text (story/poem/dialogue/procedure) with Pre-/While-/Post-reading → **A) Oral Communication** (1. Learning the Sounds, 2. Learning to Speak with a modelled dialogue) → **B) Reading and Critical Thinking** (questions, MCQ cloze, rearranging) → **C) Language Focus** (1. Vocabulary Building with English meanings + a themed set, 2. Learning to Spell — sight words in shape boxes, circle-correct-spelling, missing letters, dictation introduced, 3. Grammar with "Read Me" boxes) → **D) Writing** (1. Learning to Write, 2. Creative Writing). Teaching Point strips throughout. Reviews (printed pp.28, 58, 91, 118) recycle the same four sections.

Grade 2 deltas vs Grade 1: reading texts are longer (full-page stories), syllable work extends to 3 syllables, alphabetical order extends to the second letter, punctuation adds the exclamation mark and comma-in-list is consolidated, and new grammar appears: objective-case pronouns, has/have, articles incl. "the", weak forms and contractions of "be".

## 3. Unit-by-unit (printed page numbers)

- **Unit 1 — A Tiny Creature (p.1).** Poem "The Cricket" by Marjorie Barrows (p.3). Sounds: initial and final sounds of words; alphabet letter sounds in random order (p.4). Dialogue: introducing a younger brother (p.5). Comprehension: questions + MCQ cloze (p.6). Digraphs ch/sh/th initial and final positions (p.5). Vocabulary: bug, chirp, tug, glad, breath; rhyming away/play, tug/bug; **Parts of Body** labelled figure (hand, hair, eye, ear, teeth, neck, shoulder, elbow, finger, knee, toes, foot) (p.7). Spelling: sight words from, good, about, ride, over, your, any, blue. Grammar: **Nouns (Naming Words)** — classify into People/Places/Animals/Things (pp.8–9); **Pronouns (Substitution Words)** in subjective case (p.9). Writing: two-syllable words with syllabic division (little, perfect, money, lovely, improve); list five two-syllable words; Creative Writing: five sentences about blessings of Allah from a word bank (p.10).
- **Unit 2 — My Home and City (p.11).** Getting Started: Pakistan flag / Independence Day 14 August (p.12). Reading: Maha's first-person account of her home in Islamabad — rooms, furniture, Faisal Masjid, keeping city clean (p.13). Sounds: **Consonants and Vowels** — count vowels/consonants, mark initial vowel (V) vs consonant (C) sounds; **initial blends bl, cl, sp, pl** (block/clock/spread/plane…) (p.14). Dialogue: introducing self and father to a new neighbour (p.15). Vocabulary: airy, discuss, wide, capital; **Animal Names** — farm (cow, hen, horse) vs wild (deer, monkey, lion); pets/insects; missing letters (pp.16–17). Spelling: sight words it, of, in, was, said, with — the same set as Grade 1 Unit 3. Grammar: **Adjectives (Describing Words)** as opposite pairs (black/white, clean/dirty, heavy/light…) (p.17); **Action Words** in sentences, gap-fill from box (p.18). Writing: five sentences "My Home" from word bank; Creative Writing: five sentences about my city/village with a mind map (p.19).
- **Unit 3 — Let's Plant Trees (p.20).** Story-cum-procedure: Zain learns five steps to sow a seed from his grandfather (pp.22–23). Sounds: **digraphs sh, ch, th, ph** initial and final (shop/dish, chart/teach, thin/cloth, phone/graph) (p.23). Dialogue: borrowing a book (p.23). Vocabulary: pit, dig, sprinkle; **Fruit and Vegetable Names** (apple, mango, orange, banana, peach / carrot, tomato, brinjal, potato, onion) (p.25). Spelling: sight words right, now, just, over, your + big, went, are, come, think used in sentences. Grammar: **Singular and Plural Nouns** — now including **-es plurals** for nouns ending s, x, z, ch, o, sh (box/boxes, glass/glasses, bus/buses), change-number exercise incl. hero, watch, fox (p.26); **Position Words** on, in, under, to (p.26–27). Writing: complete a paragraph from given words; Creative Writing: "A Happy Morning" (p.27).
- **Review 1 (p.28).** Initial/final sounds; consonant vs vowel matching; role-play introductions; comprehension across Units 1–3; digraph table; meanings of airy/tug/sprinkle/pit/wide; plurals (tomato, bench, branch, glass, class, shirt); adjective opposites; naming-word columns; pronouns in sentences; circle action words in a paragraph; position-word sentences; write five sentences about your city/village (pp.28–30).
- **Unit 4 — Bee on my Nose (p.31).** Poem by R. Wayne Edwards (p.33). Sounds: **consonant clusters scr, spl, spr, str** (scrap/splash/spray/strain) (p.34); **initial and final blends** sp-, pl-, -st, -nd (p.35). Dialogue: asking teacher to discuss speech points (p.34). Vocabulary: sniff, tiff, sting (meanings table appears at the top of p.36 — see §6); rhyming crouch/ouch, sniff/tiff; **Polite Words** dialogues (Could I…? / May I…?) (p.36). Spelling: sight words very, ask, came, now, their, away, long, after. Grammar: **Pronouns as subjective case** (recap), then **Pronouns as Objective Case** — him, her, it, me, us, you, them in paired sentences (p.38); **Use of is, am, are** fill-ins (p.38). Writing: 3–5 sentences on given topics ("A Bee", "My House" starters); Creative Writing: a funny incident (pp.38–39… p.39 viewed in full).
- **Unit 5 — Attention! (p.41).** Reading: a full dialogue text — teacher and class discuss traffic lights, an accident, transport types (pp.42–43). Sounds: **Letters and Sounds** — changing the initial letter changes the word/sound ('w' to 'm': we/me; 'c' to 'f': can/fan; 's' to 'b': see/bee) (p.44). Speaking: seeking permission dialogue (p.44). Comprehension: questions; join words to make sentences; MCQ cloze (p.45). Vocabulary: transport, traffic, safely, traffic jam; **Syllables** — two- and three-syllable division table (living, famous, beautiful, favourite) with dictation note (p.46). Spelling: sight words sleep, walk, again, know, may. Grammar: **Words to show Possession** (my, his, her, your, their, its, our) (p.47); **Prepositions (Position Words)** to, from, up, down, here, there (p.47); **Articles** — a/an by sound + **"the" with the names of rivers, continents, etc.**, and an underline-the-correct-article exercise mixing a/an/the (pp.47–48). Writing: read a model dialogue, complete own dialogue in speech bubbles; Creative Writing: road safety rules from signs (p.48).
- **Unit 6 — Be Honest (p.49).** Story: Kaleem accidentally takes Asim's pencil box home, is tempted to keep it, returns it next morning (p.51). Sounds: **initial and final same sounds** — same beginning sound sets (pencil/pen/pet…) and same ending sound sets (mother/brother/sister; king/ring/bring) (p.52). Dialogue: routine greetings (Linda/Asma, exam wishes) (p.52). Comprehension: questions; classroom picture sentences (There is/are); MCQ cloze (pp.53–54). Vocabulary: together, without permission, honesty, mistake; classroom vs household objects sort (p.54). Spelling: sight words six, yellow, help, let, going (p.54). Grammar: **Masculine/Feminine** (brother/sister, uncle/aunt, father/mother, prince/princess + sort nephew, daughter, sir, woman…) (p.55); **Pronouns** — rewrite a paragraph replacing coloured nouns with pronouns (p.56); **Omission of Articles (a/an)** with plurals + insert-where-needed rewrite (p.56). Writing: list classroom objects; sentence per picture with action+describing words; Creative Writing: five lines — what would you do in Kaleem's place? (p.57).
- **Review 2 (p.58).** Change first letters; same-beginning-sound words; permission dialogue with polite words; comprehension (traffic rules, transport, colours of traffic light, Be Honest, Bee poem author); rearrange words into sentences; dictionary meanings task (sniff, transport, inquired, sting, mistake — first explicit dictionary use); pronoun paragraph rewrite; masculine/feminine sorting; position words; is/am/are paragraph; articles paragraph rewrite; write five sentences about "Bee" (pp.58–61).
- **Unit 7 — Sports Day (p.62).** Story: first-person past-tense recount of Annual Sports Day, Feb 12, 2020 — sack race win, trophy (p.64). Sounds: **Syllables** — one- vs two-syllable rhyming words (run/sun; rabbit/habit, jacket/racket) (p.65). Dialogue: introducing yourself to the class (p.65). Comprehension: questions; **clock** — big hand minutes / small hand hours, read o'clock times; **calendar** — September and December pages, days in month, Defence Day, Quaid's Day; MCQ cloze (pp.66–67). Vocabulary: compete, sack, instruction; **Colours** orange, purple, pink, black; **Shapes** rectangle, cylinder, square (printed "sqaure" — see §6), cone, crescent (p.68). Spelling: sight words use, wash, fast, light, pick. Grammar: **'Has' and 'Have'** for possession, with the singular/plural + I/you rule (p.69); **Capitalisation** — first letter of sentence and of names of people, places, pets (p.70). Writing: numbers 1–50 in words; Creative Writing: five sentences about favourite sports (p.70).
- **Unit 8 — My School (p.71).** Poem "My School" by Ruth Donnelly (p.73). Sounds: **Syllable** counting 1–3 syllables (smile; pen·cil; fam·i·ly) (p.74). Dialogue: seeking attention politely at the canteen queue (p.74). Comprehension: questions; **alphabetical order by first letter, then by second letter** (pp.75–76); brainstorming mind maps "My School" / "My Teacher" (p.76); MCQ cloze. Vocabulary: peek, loud, shiny; rhyming clean/green, be/me, while/smile; **Directions** left/right/up/down in sentences (p.77). Spelling: sight words around, after, think, those, today. Grammar: **Proper Nouns** (p.78); **Pointing Words** this/that/these/those in a singular/plural picture grid (p.78). Writing: ordinal numbers in words (p.79, pictured tens 10–100 also); short paragraph about your school; Creative Writing: paragraph "My Best Friend" (p.80).
- **Unit 9 — What a Good Deed! (p.81).** Story: Bayazid Bastami waits all night with water for his sick mother (p.83). Sounds: **irregular sight words** old, call, saw, round, fly (p.84). Dialogue: expressing likes/dislikes (school party shopping) (p.84). Comprehension: questions; **read a chart** — Ahmed's good-habits picture chart (p.85); cloze. Vocabulary: patiently, pitcher, saint, fetch; **Cardinal Numbers** one–ten with counting pictures (p.86). Spelling: sight words tell, much, keep, some, work. Grammar: **Pronouns (Revision)** — tick correct pronoun per picture; write pronoun for underlined nouns (p.87–88); **Punctuation** — full stop, question mark, exclamation mark together (p.88). Writing: ordinal-number balloon questions; ordinals first–tenth in words; date and caption format (model "9-4-2022, Friday"); Creative Writing: list of things we should not do (pp.89–90).
- **Review 3 (p.91).** Count syllables; sight words in sentences (call, old, said); likes/dislikes discussion; comprehension (sports, Sports Day date, Bayazid); alphabetical order first/second letter; meanings (sack, peek, patience, loud); this/that/these/those picture sentences; has/have paragraph; punctuation+capitalisation rewrite; "My Mother" ideas → five sentences (pp.91–93).
- **Unit 10 — An Ant and a Dove (p.94).** Fable in past tense (p.96). Sounds: **Plurals with 's', 'z' and 'iz' Sounds** — /s/ books, ants; /z/ bags, birds; /iz/ boxes, races, with IPA-style labels (p.97). Dialogue: Ant and Dove conversation (p.97). Comprehension: questions; **title and table of contents** of this very book as the exercise object; picture dictionary of birds (pp.98–99); MCQ cloze. Vocabulary: slip, pluck, scream; **Ordinal Numbers** first–tenth pictured (p.99). Spelling: sight words about, could, thought, thank, save. Grammar: **Question Words** what/who/where/when/why (p.100); **Comma (,)** — separate words in a sentence + comma after yes/no (p.101, exercise numbering slips — see §6). Writing: complete a story by replacing rebus pictures with words; Creative Writing: five sentences on "Helping Others" (p.101).
- **Unit 11 — Love for Parents (p.102).** Reading: procedure-story — Asad and Maha make a birthday card for their mother (p.104). Sounds: **Weak forms of 'a' and 'the'**; **Contractions of 'be'** — I'm, you're, he's, they're, it's, she's table with example sentences (p.105). Dialogue: favourite fruit (p.105). Comprehension: questions; a second mini-text (Zara and Neha) with **characters** defined in a Read Me; MCQ cloze (p.106). Vocabulary: fold, half, excited; **Multi-step Directions** — follow directions to make a collage (p.107). Spelling: sight words four, green, jump, every, where, take, pretty, got. Grammar: **Commands** and **Requests** (p.108 — one command example is truncated, see §6). Writing: read a model greeting card; make own card for mother; write name, phone number and address on the back; Creative Writing: sentences about your mother (p.109).
- **Unit 12 — Seasons (p.110).** Poem "Seasons" by Meish Goldish (p.112). Sounds: **Rhythm, Stress and Intonation** (p.113); body language/gestures (Hello! / Woo hoo! / oh! stick figures) (p.113). Comprehension: questions; read a seasons brochure and write a line per season; MCQ cloze (p.114). Vocabulary: greet, bud, form; rhyming snow/grow, hot/lot, cool/school; **Words to Ask Permission** — can/may dialogues (p.115). Spelling: sight words think, around, good, right, after. Grammar: **Question Words** where/who/which with answer lines (p.116). Writing: rhyming-word pairs from a spring poem + add one; short dialogue about favourite seasons; Creative Writing: paragraph on your favourite season (p.117).
- **Review 4 (p.118).** Sort plurals into /s/, /z/, /iz/ columns (ants, birds, boxes, books, watches, friends); gesture pictures; comprehension (Ant and Dove, favourite season, greeting-card materials); meanings (half, excited, scream, greet); circle commands (red) vs requests (blue); questions with what/where/which; punctuation+capitalisation rewrite incl. a comma sentence; write a dialogue about favourite foods (pp.118–119).

## 4. Grammar / vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics:** initial/final sounds + digraphs ch/sh/th (U1) → vowels/consonants + initial blends (U2) → digraphs incl. ph, initial+final (U3) → clusters scr/spl/spr/str + initial/final blends (U4) → letter-change word families (U5) → same beginning/ending sounds (U6) → syllables in rhyme (U7) → syllable counting 1–3 (U8) → irregular sight words (U9) → plural sounds /s/ /z/ /iz/ (U10) → weak forms of a/the + contractions of be (U11) → rhythm, stress, intonation (U12). A clear step beyond Grade 1: pronunciation now includes connected-speech features (weak forms, contractions) and the third plural allomorph /iz/.
- **Grammar sequence (contents grid verified on pages):** nouns + subject pronouns (U1) → adjectives (opposites) + action words (U2) → plurals incl. -es + position words (U3) → subject pronouns, **objective-case pronouns**, is/am/are (U4) → possessives, prepositions, **articles a/an/the** (U5) → masculine/feminine, pronouns in paragraph rewriting, article omission (U6) → **has/have**, capitalisation (U7) → proper nouns, pointing words (U8) → pronouns revision, **punctuation ., ?, !** (U9) → question words, **comma** (U10) → commands, requests (U11) → question words where/who/which (U12). Still no tense system: past tense occurs only as story input (Units 6, 7, 9, 10 stories are past-tense narratives, never analysed).
- **Vocabulary:** 3–5 glossed words per unit (simple English definitions) + themed sets (body parts, animals farm/wild, fruits/vegetables, colours, shapes incl. 3-D cylinder/cone/crescent, cardinals, ordinals) + ~5–8 sight words per unit (≈75 across the book, with deliberate recycling of Grade 1 sets). First dictionary task appears in Review 2 ("write their meanings with the help of a dictionary").
- **Writing:** two-syllable word writing → guided 5-sentence paragraphs with word banks and mind maps → paragraph completion → dialogue writing in speech bubbles → listing → greeting-card making with name/phone/address → story completion from rebus pictures → short dialogue writing (Review 4). Cursive is mentioned once, in Unit 11 outcomes ("make/write simple greeting cards … using cursive writing"), but no cursive models are printed in the unit.

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

- **Tenses:** No tense is named or taught. No present-simple paradigm, no past-tense formation (regular/irregular), no future. Stories supply past-tense input only.
- **Passive voice:** Absent. **Conditionals:** Absent. **Reported speech:** Absent — dialogue is practised and written as direct speech only; the one embedded-speech moment type ("she asked…") is never taught.
- **Also absent:** comparatives/superlatives (adjectives stay at naming + opposites); possessive 's (possession is possessive adjectives only, though "grandfather's garden" etc. appear untaught in texts); "the" beyond the single rule "names of rivers, continents, etc."; prepositions beyond to/from/up/down/here/there + on/in/under recycled; time expressions beyond o'clock and calendar reading; question formation as a structure (wh-words are listed and used in prompts, but no inversion or do/does); negation forms beyond don't/can't appearing in dialogues.
- **Phonics limits:** No long-vowel spelling patterns (no split digraph/magic-e, no ai/ee/oa lessons as such); vowel digraphs appear only inside sight/rhyming words; blends/clusters are initial-position except -st/-nd finals in U4.
- **Exam preparation:** None — Reviews are workbook revision, not test papers; no marks or model answers.
- **Answer keys:** None. (One partial exception in spirit: model answers are embedded in a few exercises, e.g. the first balloon ordinal is answered, "Orange is second", p.89 — but there is no key section.)
- **Also absent:** No end glossary (Review tasks re-ask meanings instead); no Urdu glosses; no listening component beyond teacher read-aloud.

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Printed p.66: the clock diagram labels are correct ("The big hand on the clock shows minutes" / "The small hand … shows hours"), but the Teaching Point on the same page reverses them: "The big hand shows the hour and the small hand shows the minutes."
- Printed p.67: MCQ asks what "was celebrated on October 12, 2019", while the Sports Day story on p.64 is dated "February 12, 2020" — the review question matches neither the story's event (Sports Day is in the options) nor its date.
- Printed p.68: shape label printed "sqaure" (for square) in the Shapes row.
- Printed p.35–36: Unit 4's "1. Vocabulary Building — i. Read the given words with their meanings" heading stands on p.35 with no table beneath it; the meanings table (sniff, tiff, sting) appears orphaned at the top of p.36 under no heading.
- Printed p.98: comprehension asks "Who was sleeping in the nest?" — nothing in the Ant and Dove story (p.96) sleeps in a nest.
- Printed p.101: within the Comma exercise the items run "iv. Read the following dialogue" then "iii. Use commas in the given sentences" — numbering repeats/reverses.
- Printed p.108: the Commands examples begin "Do your" — the sentence is cut off unfinished (next examples are complete: "Stand up.", "Shut the door.").
- Printed p.47: the articles rule is printed "We use the with the names of rivers, continents, etc." — continents do not take "the" in standard usage; as printed the rule overgeneralises.

## 7. Copy defects of this PDF file

- **Duplicated cover spread:** PDF p.1 and PDF p.126 are the same front+back cover spread — the file opens and closes with it (same pattern as Grade 1).
- **Watermark:** Every page carries the diagonal "Web version of PCTB Textbook / Not for Sale" watermark; the sparse text layer is again dominated by this watermark (texts/g02.txt is largely watermark fragments plus the credits print-run table).
- **Folio continuity:** Printed folios run 1–119 continuously, no missing/duplicated/out-of-order content pages; unit start pages match the contents grid exactly (U1 p.1, U2 p.11, U3 p.20, R1 p.28, U4 p.31, U5 p.41, U6 p.49, R2 p.58, U7 p.62, U8 p.71, U9 p.81, R3 p.91, U10 p.94, U11 p.102, U12 p.110, R4 p.118). No blank or truncated pages in the PDF itself. (One page image was corrupted in my local render by a disk-full incident and was re-rendered from the PDF — the PDF page itself is intact.)
