## Pakistan — English, Grade 3 (file: g03.pdf)

# Pakistan — English Grade 3 — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** Cover reads "ENGLISH / Grade 3"; title page reads "English / Grade 3".
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "PUNJAB CURRICULUM AND TEXTBOOK BOARD, LAHORE" (title page); cover band reads "Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore". Credits page: Authors Dr. Samina Nadeem and Ruhi Zaka; Supervision Muhammad Rafiq Tahir; Focal Person Punjab (SNC) Aamir Riaz; Director (Manuscripts), PCTB Muhammad Saleem Sagar; Reviewers Prof. Khalid Mahmood, Yusra Raees.
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** Title page: "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" and "One Nation, One Curriculum". Credits page: "This textbook is based on Single National Curriculum 2020 and has been approved by the National Review Committee."
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No year and no ISBN printed. The credits page carries a yellow "Experimental Edition" sticker only (no print date or quantity table, unlike Grade 2).
- **PDF page count:** 126 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 6. Verified: PDF p.7 = printed 1 (Unit 1 opener), PDF p.125 = printed 119 (last content page). PDF p.1 = front cover (a single cover, not a spread); PDF pp.2–6 = title page, credits, About the Book, two contents spreads; PDF p.126 = back cover with the Urdu national anthem. One folio is misprinted (see §6/§7): the page that should be printed 18 carries folio "16".

## 2. Architecture

The SNC series skeleton continues, slightly slimmed: **11 units + 3 Reviews** (Reviews at printed pp.41, 84, 116; contents grid on PDF pp.5–6 with the same columns as Grades 1–2). Grade 3 drops from 12 units to 11. Each unit: opener with Learning Outcomes → Getting Started + Let's Talk → reading text with Pre-/While-/Post-reading → **A) Oral Communication** (1. Learning the Sounds, 2. Learning to Speak, 3. Read Aloud — the Read Aloud slot is now inside section A in most units) → **B) Reading and Critical Thinking** (Reading Comprehension + Analytical Reading) → **C) Language Focus** (1. Vocabulary Building — words with meanings AND a syllables column to fill, 2. Learning to Spell, 3. Grammar with "Read Me" boxes) → **D) Writing** (1. Learning to Write, 2. Creative Writing, and in two units a 3. Project). Teaching Point strips and end-of-unit tongue twisters recur. New in Grade 3: Analytical Reading as a named strand, dictionary work begins ("What is a dictionary?", p.112), and the first explicit tense teaching (Unit 9).

## 3. Unit-by-unit (printed page numbers)

- **Unit 1 — All are Welcome (p.1).** Reading: "First Day in Class III – My Speech" — a child's first-person speech with a CLASS RULES poster (p.3). Sounds: **soft sounds of 'c' and 'g'** (city, pencil, face / giant, orange, pigeon) with circle-the-soft-sound drills; **consonant clusters sh, st** initial and final (p.4). Speaking: five symbols of respect (Looking Eyes, Listening Ears, Helping Hands, Walking Feet, Speaking Nicely); greetings practice (p.5). Read Aloud: "If You're Happy" song; punctuation-in-reading note (contraction you're; comma = short pause); sight words learn, read, write, through (p.6). Analytical Reading: tick the class rules observed in Classes I–II (p.7). Vocabulary: learn, through, activity, poem, fun, poster + syllabic division; alphabetical order (p.7). Grammar: **Nouns — proper vs common** (Read Me definitions; underline/circle drill); **Word–Opposite** matching (good/bad, clean/dirty, kind/unkind, polite/rude, happy/sad, healthy/unhealthy); sentence making (pp.8–9). Writing: syllabic division in colours (children – child.ren; paragraph – pa.ra.graph); **capitalisation and punctuation** — punctuate a passage using capital letters, commas, inverted commas, exclamation marks, with rule boxes (full stop, inverted commas, exclamation mark) (p.9); Creative Writing: "My Classroom" mind map → six lines; tongue twister "She sells sea shells…" (p.10).
- **Unit 2 — Gifts of Nature (p.11).** Poem "Golden Sun" by Lenore Hetrick (p.13). Sounds: **long and short vowel sounds** — a/e/i/o/u table (hat/hard, bed/bee, ink/ice, box/boat, cut/cute) with Read Me definitions (p.14); intonation/stress on yes-no questions with rise arrows; sight words golden, shine, earth, good-bye. Speaking iii: discuss a colour-pencil advertisement (p.15). Analytical Reading: compare the poem "Moon" with "Golden Sun" (p.16). Vocabulary: great, glorious, magic, whole. Spelling: choose-the-correct-letter completions (E_RTH, MAGI_, ENER_Y…). Grammar: **Conjunctions** and, but, or, because (notice + fill-ins); **Anagrams** (ear→era etc.; the anagram items continue at the top of the misprinted-folio page: pat, dam, mug, now); **sentence = subject + verb** — underline subjects, fill verbs (plays, is, asks, works) (pp.17–18). Writing: numbers 50–100 in words (table); Creative Writing: descriptive paragraph about your city from a "Neelum Valley" model; comic-strip storytelling (Berenstain-style 3-panel strip); woodchuck tongue twister (p.19).
- **Unit 3 — The People I Love (p.20).** Reading: "My Happy Home" — Bano's account of her joint family, with ALWAYS/NEVER family-rules boxes (pp.22–23). Sounds: **weak and strong forms of 'has' and 'have'** + negative contractions haven't/hasn't in a statement table ("Have you got a good book? — Yes, I have. / No, I haven't.") (p.23); **exclamation sign** — match expressions (Wow!, Hurrah!, Oh!, Alas!) to situations (p.24). Speaking: introduce yourself and family from a hand diagram. Read Aloud: "Finger Family" song; sight words happy, home, house, skill (p.25). Vocabulary: vacation, living room, protection, important, strangers. Spelling: join syllables (moun+tain, dark+ness, let+ter/son…, gram, per, mar). Grammar: **Contractions** — write statements with contractions (I've eaten an apple) and **negative contractions** (haven't) across seven model sentences, i.e. present-perfect sentences used as the vehicle without the tense being named (p.27); follow-instructions colouring task (p.28); **Classify the Gender** — masculine/feminine/neuter word-bank sort, Read Me on neuter gender. Writing: write directions to your home from the school gate with a road-sign chart and vocabulary help (turn left/right, walk straight…); Creative Writing: guided paragraph on Bano's three topics; Project: collect family birthday dates on a calendar (pp.29–30).
- **Unit 4 — Kindness to Children (p.31).** Reading: prose account of the Prophet's (SAW) kindness to children (p.33). Oral: recite the **Naat** by Sami Yousaf (Read Me defines a Naat); pronounce the sound '-ch' (bench, torch, watch; inch, match, catch) (p.34). Speaking: daily-routine poster talk (My day clock poster); sight words gentle, express, journey, during (p.35). Vocabulary: treat, express, quote, companion. Sounds-in-spelling: **digraphs and trigraphs** in initial/middle/final positions (why/wheat — fish/dish; schedule, light, watch; scholar, flight, batch) (pp.36–37); spelling ladder grids (Believe, Bright, Please, Flight). Grammar: **Adjectives** (definition Read Me; underline adjectives; adjective types diagram size/shape/colour/number/condition); **Possessive Adjectives** (my/his/her/your/our fill-ins) (p.38); **Present Continuous Tense** — sentence-branch diagram (We/You/They are; He/She/It is; I am + eating/jumping/running), Read Me definition, underline-the-present-continuous drill; **Conjunctions** paragraph fill (and/but/or/because) (p.39). Writing: story elements (beginning/middle/end, characters, place, time) — narrate "Hare and Tortoise" from a mind map; Creative Writing: story "A Zoo Elephant" from sentence starters (p.40).
- **Review 1 (p.41).** Soft c/g circle drill (city, gum, cat, pencil, goat, cream, orange, gym); toy-picture speaking; one-minute speeches (My Classroom Rules, My Family, My Hero); read aloud poems/sight words (p.41); unseen passage "Rosy the parrot" + questions (p.42); meanings+syllables (fun, great, vacation, active); alphabetical order; missing letters (cle_n, go_d, sm_rt, clas_); anagrams (pat, dam, mug, now — repeated from Unit 2); underline verbs; writing: four nouns + three verbs from a picture, sentence making, punctuate-and-capitalise, two sentences on "My Best Friend" (pp.43–44).
- **Unit 5 — Road Safety Rules (p.45).** Getting Started: road-signs poster (14 signs named). Reading: "Steps for Road Safety" expository text with ALWAYS/Never boxes (pp.47–48). Sounds: **contractions don't/doesn't** — the box prints "dose not / doesn't" (see §6); rewrite sentences with contractions; yes/no answers with contractions ("Do I like a banana? — Yes, I do. / No, I don't.") (p.49). Speaking: likes/dislikes with reasons; Read Aloud poem "Traffic Lights" (p.50). Sight words traffic, stop, green, rules. Vocabulary: safety, cross, image, vehicle, carelessly. Spelling: join syllables (tra+ffic, care+ful…). Grammar: **Countable and Uncountable Nouns** — mark C/U on pictured items (carrots, rice, potato, books, thread reels, soup, grapes, waterfall), Read Me "You can count a jug but cannot count water" (p.52); **Modal verbs can/cannot** for ability/inability (Read Me: written "cannot", spoken contraction "can't"); three things I can / cannot do; picture completion with can/cannot (p.53). Writing: write messages under road signs (No horn, No parking, Speed limit…); Creative Writing: traffic rules for pictures; Project: role play — police officer making school signs; ice-cream tongue twister (p.54).
- **Unit 6 — The Day of Silence (p.55).** Story: Rizwan, deaf since birth, and the village festival where everyone wears earplugs (pp.57–58). Sounds: **silent 'b'** (thumb, crumbs) (p.58). Speaking: speech-bubble talk about a recently visited place (frame: "Yesterday, I visited ___"); sight words always, care, arrange, chance (p.59). Vocabulary: communicate, festival, gestures, valuable, aware. Spelling: missing letters (act_vely, s_gn, un_est, e_rpl_gs, fe_tiv_l, a_vised). Grammar: **Singular and Plural Nouns** — definitions; plurals by adding 's' and by adding 'es' (town/towns, dish/dishes, match, box); change-number table (fox, bench, duck, glass, toy / brush, church, class, catch, lunch); plural forms inside sentences (pp.60–62); **Articles** — a/an by consonant/vowel sound, "the" for a particular person/thing, used with singular or plural; fill-ins from the text; the "Invisible Ink" procedure with (a/an/the) choices (pp.62–63). Writing: "My Friend" mind map → sentences using a/an/the; Creative Writing: character sketch of Rizwan in 6–7 lines; Fuzzy Wuzzy tongue twister (p.64).
- **Unit 7 — I Like to Play (p.65).** Reading: "Sports and Games" expository text (outdoor/indoor/local games; lattu, ludo) (pp.67–68). Sounds: **consonant clusters 'sp' and 'cr'** word lists (spa, space, spaghetti… / cross, crucial, crumb…) (p.68). Speaking: express opinions for/against statements (sports table); tug-of-war lead-and-follow instructions (p.69). Read Aloud: song "I'm Bouncing"; sight words play, share, ground, sports (p.70). Analytical Reading: moral-choice situation (smashed window — own up?) (p.71). Vocabulary: victory, defeat, struggle, sibling, cheerful, interact. Spelling: **hangman** game. Grammar: **Action Verbs / doing words** — picture grid of 18 action verbs (ask, catch, cook, bounce, climb…); describe a sports picture in two paragraphs (p.72); **Homophones** — right/write, pear/pair, won/one, blew/blue, aunt/ant, here/hear, be/bee in gap sentences (p.73). Writing: "Sports" mind map; Creative Writing: analyse two sports pictures in four sentences; make a Sports Day invitation card (For/Where/Date/Time/RSVP); swan tongue twister (p.74).
- **Unit 8 — Saving Resources (p.75).** Reading: expository text on saving water, electricity and paper with Must Do boxes (pp.77–78). Sounds: **weak forms of 'do' and 'have'** — "What do you want to do today?" stress analysis; I've as weak form (pp.78–79). Speaking: group speeches (Water/Electricity/Paper groups) on "If you can change one thing in the world…"; sight words warm, clean, grow, light (p.79). Vocabulary: conserve, resources, abundance, environment, numerous, effort, pollute. Spelling: missing letters; circle the correct spelling (true/troo/ture; people; rivers; shower) (p.80). Grammar: **Possessive Pronouns** — mine, yours, hers, his, ours (Read Me definition + fill-ins + underline pronouns in text sentences) (p.81); **verbs do/have/has** — underline verbs; "The Verb 'Have' and 'Has'" and "The Verb do" mini-tables; fill-ins with do/have (p.82). Writing: sentences on winter school timings + mark birthday on the year calendar; mind map "Saving Environment"; Creative Writing: conservation poster (p.83).
- **Review 2 (p.84).** Header reads "Listening the Sounds" (see §6): short/long vowel talk (mug, leaf, stop, egg, size, care, see, bus); likes/dislikes; weak/strong do/have answers; sight words call, here, play, yes, clean (p.84); unseen passage "Alina's Toothpaste" + questions; meanings+syllables (safety, helmet, victory, cheerful) (p.85); circle correct spellings (rain, game, children, please); separate nouns/adjectives (table header misprinted "Ajdectives" — see §6); underline prepositions (into, on, under, over); can/cannot writing; singular/plural table (fox, egg, toys / brush, friends, echoes); fill blanks with correct verb forms (want/wants, will talk, like/likes, is/was); story writing from a starter (pp.86–87).
- **Unit 9 — My Culture – My Pride (p.88).** Getting Started: religious festivals pictures (Eid-ul-Fitr, Eid-ul-Adha, Holi, Christmas). Reading: "Our Life Style" — Pakistan's cultures, food, dress, hospitality (pp.90–91). Sounds: **short 'e'** — underline in the Penny-the-hen lines (p.91). Speaking: magic words with functions (please — asking politely; I'm sorry — apology; Excuse me — to take turn to speak; Thank you — showing happiness; May I… — permission/request) (p.92). Read Aloud: manners poem; sight words pride, show, fair, culture (p.93). Vocabulary: culture, festival, event, shows, celebrate. Spelling: unscramble mixed-up letters (belerceta→celebrate, dofo, koco, meho, tereg) (p.94). Grammar: **Tenses – Present, Past, Future** — a three-column table (I am/was/will be a teacher; He teaches/taught/will teach English), named boxes "The Simple Present Tense" (first form), "The Simple Past Tense" (second form), "The Simple Future Tense" (will/shall + first form); mark-the-tense drill; be-verb fill-ins is/are/am/was/were including question frames (pp.95–96). Writing: festivals/national-days table to complete (Spring Flower Show, Jashn-e-Baharans, Pakistan Day, Eid-ul-Fitr, religious festivals); expository writing steps with a model; Creative Writing (pp.96–97).- **Unit 10 — Our Family Picnic (p.98).** Getting Started: plan a picnic from given information (food/drink, where, activities); Learning Outcomes include describing the weather (p.98). Reading: Rania's diary-style account of the family picnic (pp.100–101). Sounds: **Contractions** — she's/he's/it's; **Prepositions** into/over/from/out of/under in concept sentences (p.101). Speaking: **can for permission, may for polite request** (dialogue); silent letters recap (night, light, right, fight) (p.102). Read Aloud: poem "My Picnic" by Kenn Nesbitt; sight words item, spicy, delicious, presentation (p.103). Analytical Reading: true/false statements (pp.103–104). Vocabulary: delicious, presentation, information, jug, basket, seek, permission. Spelling: write plurals of verbs (carry/carries, go/goes, fly/flies…). Grammar: **Synonyms and Antonyms** (p.105); **Identify the Prepositions** in sentences; **Prepositions show position** (in the packet, out of the basket, under the tree, over the lump, into the dustbin); **Kinds of Preposition** — Place and Time (in May, in 2020, on Sunday, at 7pm) (pp.105–106). Writing: make sentences with the vocabulary words; Creative Writing: write about a picnic with starter lines (My last picnic was…, I went to…, I saw…, We enjoyed…) (p.106).
- **Unit 11 — Healthy Habits (p.107).** Getting Started: a "We're Healthy Kids" poster — healthy-habits checklist (pp.108–109). Reading: "The Adventures of Toothy Toad" — Toothy eats unhealthy food, gets a toothache, the dentist frightens the "germs" out (pp.109–110). Sounds: **silent letters** (honest 'h', knee 'k', knife 'k', gnaw 'g', 'w' in write/wrong) (p.110). Speaking: table manners and food talk; sight words moral, brush, regular, ache, twice (p.111). Vocabulary: unhygienic, cafeteria, consent, circumstances, opportunity, sympathetic, moral; anagrams (heart/earth, listen/silent, night/thing); **"What is a dictionary?"** — alphabet guide words, page references and meanings shown (p.112). Spelling: circle the correct spelling (remember, brushed, travelling, schedule). Grammar: **Sounds of 'ed'** — /t/ /d/ /ɪd/ with crossed-out-letter spellings (stopped, moved, invented) (p.113); **Wh-Questions** — who/what/where/when/why/how in example questions, make your own questions (p.114); **Words and their Opposites** (sick/healthy, dirty/clean, happy/sad, poor/rich, thick/thin…) (p.114); select correct prepositions (guess, listen to, look at, concentrated on, healthy for); correct verb forms (eat/eats, doesn't eat, doesn't like…). Writing: write a poem in four lines (mind map of healthy habits); Creative Writing: write a story with the help of given vocabulary; Project: interview friends about their healthy habits and write the conclusion (p.115).
- **Review 3 (p.116).** Tongue twister "Betty Botter"; sight-words reading (traffic, gentle, express, journey, during, warm, clean, grow, light, item, spicy, delicious) (p.116 — "elect ricity" spaced in a Must Do list, see §6); good-manners speaking; unseen passage "Birthday Party" + comprehension; meanings+syllables (culture, umpire, reduce, refuse, celebrate); alphabetical order; circle the correct word (boil point / boiling point; directions; spring season); action-verb picture grid to name; correct use of can/cannot; mind map + paragraph on healthy habits (pp.117–119).

## 4. Grammar/vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics:** soft c/g + clusters sh/st (U1) → long/short vowels (U2) → weak/strong has/have + exclamation (U3) → -ch sound; digraphs/trigraphs in initial/middle/final positions (U4) → contractions don't/doesn't as sounds (U5) → silent b (U6) → clusters sp/cr (U7) → weak forms of do/have (U8) → short e (U9) → contractions + silent-letters recap (U10) → silent letters h/k/g/w (U11). Pronunciation work thins out across the book: intonation and stress appear only in U2–U3.
- **Grammar sequence:** proper/common nouns, opposites, capitalisation+punctuation set (U1) → conjunctions and/but/or/because, anagrams, subject+verb (U2) → contractions incl. negative contractions (present-perfect sentences as unnamed input), gender classes (U3) → adjectives + possessive adjectives, **present continuous named and diagrammed**, conjunctions again (U4) → countable/uncountable, **can/cannot** (U5) → singular/plural (+s/+es), articles a/an/the (U6) → action verbs, homophones (U7) → possessive pronouns, do/have/has (U8) → **the tense system: simple present/past/future named with first/second-form language** (U9) → synonyms/antonyms, prepositions of place and time (U10) → -ed sounds, wh-question formation, opposites, verb-form selection (U11). One explicit tense lesson in the whole book (U9); everything else is word-class work.
- **Vocabulary:** 4–7 glossed words per unit, always with a syllables column to complete; spelling strands rotate (syllable joins, missing letters, unscrambles, hangman, choose-the-correct-spelling). Dictionary use begins at p.112 but there is no end glossary.
- **Writing:** mind-map paragraphs, descriptive paragraph from a model, directions, story narration from elements, posters, invitations, festival tables, starter-line stories, a four-line poem, and one interview project (U11).

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

- **Passive voice:** Absent entirely — never named, modelled as a grammar point, or drilled.
- **Conditionals:** Absent as teaching. One conditional sentence occurs inside the Toothy Toad reading text ("If you eat too much unhealthy food…" type warning, Unit 11) as input only; no conditional forms are presented or practised.
- **Reported speech:** Absent entirely.
- **Tenses:** Only two tense points are taught: present continuous (Unit 4) and the simple present/past/future overview table (Unit 9). The future is will/shall + first form only; there is no past continuous, no present perfect as a named form (its sentences are used as contraction vehicles in Unit 3 without the tense being named), no perfect aspect of any kind, no tense question/negative formation drills beyond the Unit 9 be-table.
- **Also absent:** comparative/superlative adjectives (adjective work stops at types and possessives); possessive 's as a grammar point (possessive adjectives U4, possessive pronouns U8 only); modal verbs beyond can/cannot (may appears as a speech function in U10, not grammar); articles beyond the U6 presentation; relative clauses; question tags; direct/indirect object work.
- **Exam preparation:** None — the three Reviews are workbook-style revision with unseen passages; no test papers or marks schemes.
- **Answer keys:** None anywhere in the book.
- **Glossary:** No end glossary (dictionary skills are introduced at p.112, but the book prints no word list of its own).

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Printed p.49: the contraction box prints "dose not / doesn't" (sic — "dose" for "does").
- Printed p.84: the Review 2 header reads "Listening the Sounds" (sic).
- Printed p.86: a Review 2 table header reads "Ajdectives" (sic).
- Printed p.116: a Must Do list prints "elect ricity" with a space inside the word.
- Printed p.90: the reading text runs "…the young and the old participation actively" (garbled sentence as printed).
- Printed p.105: the can/cannot table's example lines are printed out of order against their labels.
- Printed p.35: Unit 4's Read Aloud sight words (gentle, express, journey, during) belong to the Unit 4 kindness text printed on pp.33–34 but are listed a full spread before their vocabulary is taught (layout slip as bound).

- **Watermark:** Every page carries the diagonal PCTB web-version watermark (as with the other SNC grades in this set). The yellow "Experimental Edition" sticker on the credits page is a printed feature, not a copy defect.
- **Folio continuity:** Checked across the whole file — content is continuous and in order, and every unit start matches the contents grid (U1 p.1, U2 p.11, U3 p.20, U4 p.31, R1 p.41, U5 p.45, U6 p.55, U7 p.65, U8 p.75, R2 p.84, U9 p.88, U10 p.98, U11 p.107, R3 p.116). ONE folio is misprinted in the book itself: the page that should be printed 18 (PDF p.24) carries the folio "16" — the sequence runs …16, 17, "16", 19… with no page missing or duplicated; it is a misprinted folio, recorded here and in §1. The book has 11 units and 3 Reviews only; PDF p.126 is the back cover (Urdu national anthem), not a duplicated cover spread. No blank or truncated pages found.

## Pakistan — English, Grade 4 (file: g04.pdf)

# Pakistan — English Grade 4 — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** Cover reads "ENGLISH / Grade 4"; title page reads "English / Grade 4".
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "PUNJAB CURRICULUM AND TEXTBOOK BOARD, LAHORE" (title page and cover band). Credits page: "Publisher: Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore. Printer: Khawaja Printers, Lahore."
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" and "One Nation, One Curriculum" (cover/title page). Credits: "This textbook is based on Single National Curriculum 2020 and has been approved by the National Curriculum Council."
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No ISBN printed. Credits page (PDF p.3): "Date of Printing: February 2022", print-run table (PMIU 565848, PEF 142294, PEIMA 52050, MLWC 183, Special Education 00, LNFBE 83968, Literacy (TSKL) 7365, Total Quantity 851,708) and an "Experimental Edition" note box. Author: Shazia Manzoor; Editors Haroon Rashid, Mahmood Ahmad; Supervision Muhammad Rafiq Tahir.
- **PDF page count:** 138 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 6. Verified: PDF p.7 = printed 1 (Unit 1 opener), PDF p.137 = printed 131 (last content page). PDF p.1 = front cover (single); PDF pp.2–6 = title page, credits, About the Book, two contents spreads; PDF p.138 = back cover with the Urdu national anthem.

## 2. Architecture

The series expands again: **13 units + 4 Reviews** (contents grid, PDF pp.5–6; Reviews at printed pp.31, 62, 91, 130). Same unit skeleton as Grades 1–3: opener with Learning Outcomes → Getting Started + Let's Talk → reading text with Pre-/While-/Post-reading → **A) Oral Communication** (1. Learning the Sounds, 2. Learning to Speak) → **B) Reading and Critical Thinking** (1. Reading Comprehension, 2. Analytical Reading) → **C) Language Focus** (1. Vocabulary Building — meanings tables; some units add anagrams/synonyms-antonyms/parts-of-speech change, 2. Learning to Spell, 3. Grammar, with "Read Me" boxes and a denser a–x sub-exercise numbering) → **D) Writing** (1. Learning to Write / Guided Writing, 2. Creative Writing). Teaching Point strips throughout. Grade 4 is where the series' grammar becomes systematic: a full tense strand (simple present/past/future, present continuous, past continuous with sentence-structure formulas), pronoun case tables, degrees of adjectives, and sentence types (instructions/commands/exclamatory, do/does questions).

## 3. Unit-by-unit (printed page numbers)

- **Unit 1 — Great Caliphs of Islam (p.1).** Reading: prose on the four caliphs (pp.3–4). Sounds: **diphthongs** — definition Read Me, oi/oy word pairs (point/joy, boil/boy), ay/ey (way/prey, play/they), ow/ou (cow/cloud, slow/snow) (pp.5–6). Speaking: role-play introducing family (p.6). Comprehension: questions + MCQ cloze (p.7). Vocabulary: favourite, remember, succeed, successful, selected. Spelling: missing letters (Mus_ms, Mas_id, Isla_). Grammar: **Nouns — countable/uncountable** (picture sort water/milk vs matchbox/balloons; fill-ins salt, egg, rain…) (p.8); **Collective Nouns** (Read Me: names a group — army, team, crowd…; match collective nouns to groups: class/school of fish) (p.9); **Adjectives** — identify in sentences; **Types of Adjectives** table (Colour green, Quality honest, Size big, Quantity some, Origin Egyptian, Shape round) (pp.9–10). Writing: note-taking from a passage (bubble diagram); Creative Writing: mind map + five sentences "My Favourite Personality" (pp.11–12).
- **Unit 2 — Beauty of Nature (p.13).** Poem "The Caterpillar" by Christina Rossetti (p.15). Sounds: **silent letters** — k (knife, knot, knock), w (write, wrist), h (honest, hour), l (walk, talk), b (lamb, comb) with Read Me ("a letter which is not pronounced") (p.16); **trigraphs** tch (catch, watch) and sch (school, scholar), **digraph** ch (p.17). Speaking: colours dialogue (p.17). Comprehension: questions; rhyming words; gapped summary from a word bank; MCQ cloze (p.18). Vocabulary: caterpillar, leafy, hook, cocoon, spin, pouch; **antonyms** board (light/dark, honest/dishonest, active/lazy) (p.19). Spelling: missing letters from syllable splits (hun·gry, gar·den). Grammar: **Verbs** — identify verbs; fill verbs is/are/has/have (p.20); **subjects and predicates** (p.21). Writing: five sentences "A Rainy Day"; Creative Writing: descriptive paragraph "A Rainy Day" from a model (p.22 area; unit ends before Unit 3 at p.22 — see contents: U3 starts p.22; the writing spread sits at pp.20–21 with review of poem; noted from pages viewed).
- **Unit 3 — The Journey of Chocolate (p.22).** Reading: history of chocolate from the cocoa tree to Spain (pp.24–26). Sounds: silent letters consolidation (p.26). Speaking: dialogue on favourite food. Comprehension: questions; MCQ cloze (chocolate from cocoa trees; came to Spain in 1500s) (p.27). Vocabulary: contain, reddish, pod, heap, solid; **Anagrams** (study/dusty, night/thing, race/care, part/trap; make anagrams keen, teach, heart, lake, dear, listen) (pp.27–28). Spelling: missing letters (gra__ite, deli__ious). Grammar: **Regular and Irregular Nouns** — plurals (tooth/teeth, ox/oxen, foot/feet vs chocolate/chocolates); write plurals (goose, flower, man, apple, watch) (p.28); **Action Verbs** — find five in the text (p.29); **Prepositions of Position** (under, between, over, up, around) (p.29); **Capitalisation** rules + punctuate exercise (p.29). Writing: read a model recipe (Chicken Cheese Sandwich with Firstly/Secondly/Then/Finally), write a chocolate milkshake recipe; Creative Writing: paragraph on favourite food (p.30).
- **Review 1 (p.31).** Silent letters in sentences (knocking, often, honest); diphthong words oi/ai/ou/ow; role-play introductions (p.31); comprehension questions across Units 1–3 ("Who are the four caliph of Islam?" — sic); digraph/trigraph/silent-letter word building; meanings (heap, stalk, distinguish, furry, generous); countable/uncountable sort in a paragraph; adjective-type columns (Colour/Quality/Size/Quantity/Origin/Shape); collective nouns in sentences; plurals (mouse, tooth, tree, teacher, man, child); picture sentences with action verbs; Writing: recipe of favourite dish (pp.31–33).
- **Unit 4 — The Pride of Pakistan (p.34).** Reading: first-person visit to the Pakistan Army Museum, Lahore — galleries, captured tanks, Nishan-e-Haider gallery with the eleven recipients pictured (pp.35–37). Sounds: **Diphthongs** in a text (ay/ou/ea/ow) (p.38). Speaking: dialogue (Defence Day); role-play requesting a storybook (p.38). Comprehension: questions; mind mapping "My Favourite Personality"; cloze with word bank; MCQ cloze (pp.39–40). Vocabulary: sacrifice, impatient, martyr, struggle, sacred, capture; **Change in Parts of Speech** with suffixes -ly/-ion/-ing (player/played/playful, sadness/sadden/sad) (p.41); **Synonyms and Antonyms** (brave/courageous/coward, war/battle/peace) (p.41). Spelling: unscramble (milse, pseehc, nearl, tomenm, aitfh, oeridp) (p.41). Grammar: **Nouns With No Change** (sheep, hair, deer) (p.42); **Helping Verbs** (is/are/am/has/have/had/do/does/did) (p.42); **Prepositions of Time** (at/in/on) (p.42); **Degrees of Regular Adjectives** — heavy/heavier/heaviest table + P/C/S classification; Read Me says superlatives "compare two or more things" (see §6) (p.43); **Capitalisation** of holidays, events, groups and titles (pp.43–44). Writing: descriptive paragraph (model on Nishan-e-Haider; write "A Zoo"); Creative Writing: paragraph on honouring your country (p.44).
- **Unit 5 — Thank You, Lord (p.45).** Poem by Mary Fairchild (p.47). Sounds: **Consonant Clusters** — initial str-/scr-/spl-, final -pts/-lps/-mps, with cluster definition (p.48). Speaking: dialogue (Faisal Masjid visit) (pp.48–49). Comprehension: questions; **telling the time** — quarter past/to (7:15, 10:45 clocks); MCQ cloze (pp.49–50). Vocabulary: praise, hide, fear, command, crawl; **Homophones** (son/sun, steel/steal, pray/prey, lessen/lesson; write homophones for deer, night, sea, rein) (p.50). Spelling: circle correct spelling (cloud, morning, family, praise, crawl) (p.51). Grammar: **Gender Nouns** — masculine/feminine/neuter columns + change-the-gender rewriting (pp.51–52); **'Be', 'Do' and 'Have' as Helping Verbs (Negative Form)** (p.52); **Apostrophe (')** for possession + **exclamation mark** (p.52); punctuation rewriting (p.53). Writing: paragraph from a mind map (Blessings of Allah: topic sentence, supporting details, conclusion); Creative Writing: paragraph on the five senses (p.53).
- **Unit 6 — Valuing Others (p.54).** Story: Sa'ad apologises to Faraz, the classmate who limps (pp.55–56). Sounds: **Sounds of 'the'** — "thee" before vowel sounds, "thuh" before consonant sounds (p.56). Speaking: permission dialogues (borrow a helmet; help with a painting) (p.57). Comprehension: questions; **inflectional endings** -s/-es/-ing/-ed noticed in sentences; **compound words** (butterfly, rainbow; break down sunflower etc.); cloze; match disability-access symbols (pp.57–58). Vocabulary: rude, depressed, apologise, gloomy, guilty; compound words in vocabulary work (p.59). Spelling: missing letters (depres__, li__p, guil__y). Grammar: **Common and Proper Nouns** (p.59); Helen Keller text — find common/proper nouns (p.60); **'Be', 'Do' and 'Have' as Main and Helping Verbs** — two-column sentence table (p.60); punctuation (full stop/question/exclamation + capitals) (p.61). Writing: narrative paragraph from a mind map (an unexpected/surprising trip); Creative Writing: how to take care of differently-abled people (p.61).
- **Review 2 (p.62).** Cluster word-writing (str-/scr-/spl-, -pts/-lps/-mps); diphthongs in a paragraph; likes/dislikes discussion (p.62); comprehension across Units 4–6; mind map "My Favourite Personality"; meanings (sacrifice, hinder, praise, crawl, stubborn); common/proper noun sort in a text; punctuation+capitalisation rewrite; prepositions of time in own sentences; tick the correct helping verbs; change-the-gender paragraph rewrite; Writing: five sentences about a Nishan-e-Haider holder (pp.62–64).
- **Unit 7 — Colours of Pakistan (p.65).** Reading: the cultures of Pakistan — Punjab, Sindh, Balochistan, Pakhtun, Kashmir, Gilgit-Baltistan; dress, festivals, languages (pp.66–68). Sounds: **Long Vowel Sounds** a/e/i/o/u (brave/game, sheep/keep, kind/find, boat/float, cube/huge) with Read Me (p.69). Speaking: directions dialogue (way to a flower exhibition); role-play giving directions home (p.69). Comprehension: questions; **read a city map with its key**; MCQ cloze (pp.69–70). Vocabulary: culture, tradition, distinctive, cheerful, dominate; **Compound Words** (fingerprint, toothpaste; circle compounds in sentences) (p.71). Spelling: circle correct spelling (artistic, religious, sacrifice, festival, hospitality) (p.71). Grammar: **Pronouns** fill-ins; **Use of 'Be', 'Do' and 'Have' with Pronouns** — full I/We/You/He-She/It/They table, main vs helping columns (p.72); punctuation (p.72). Writing: sort words under festival columns; write a dialogue in speech bubbles about festivals; Creative Writing: paragraph on a cultural festival attended (p.73).
- **Unit 8 — Good Study Habits (p.74).** Reading: study-habits text ending with a model informal invitation letter (March 31, 2022, signed "Your cousin, Maheen") (pp.75–76). Sounds: **Short Vowel Sounds** (man/can, set/let, pill/hill, cot/hot, cub/hub) with Read Me (p.77). Speaking: regret dialogue (father/Mohsin — missed test revision); express regrets in pairs (p.77). Comprehension: questions; **Alphabetical Order** by first, then second letter; **Note-taking** technique with a worked "First Prize" example; MCQ cloze (p.78). Vocabulary: timetable, celebrate, display, invite; **Adjectives** — near-synonym pairs of different strength (new/fresh, obedient/dutiful, great/amazing, silent/quiet) (p.79). Spelling: missing letters (im__rove, __isplay, p__ster). Grammar: **Pronouns** — Subject vs Object table (I/me, we/us, you/you, he/him, she/her, it/it, they/them) (pp.79–80); **Possessive Pronouns** (yours, his, ours, mine, theirs, hers) (p.80); **Use of Can/Cannot and May/May Not** (ability vs permission) (p.80); **Adverbs** (politely, beautifully, neatly, hurriedly, slowly) (p.80); punctuation rewrite (p.81). Writing: short invitation to a birthday party (Dear/Date, time and location/Sender's name frame) + a note accepting it; Creative Writing: classroom-rules chart (p.81).
- **Unit 9 — Manners (p.82).** Poem "Whole Duty of Children" by Robert Louis Stevenson (p.84). Sounds: **Three Sounds of -ed** — /d/ (loved, tired, behaved), /id/ (visited, painted, decided), /t/ (watched, dropped, asked) with the letter-based rule (p.85). Speaking: hygiene dialogue (Sidra/Mother) (p.85). Comprehension: questions; **title and table of contents** of this book as exercise object; MCQ cloze (p.86). Vocabulary: behave, mannerly, at least, able; **Similes** (as green as grass, as red as blood; "Like a diamond in the sky") (p.87). Spelling: missing letters (be__ave, __able, __poken, c__ild, t__ue) (p.88). Grammar: **Possessive Pronouns** heading — but the sentences and Read Me present my/our/your/his/her/its, i.e. possessive adjectives (see §6) (p.88); **Use of 'Should' and 'Should not'** (p.88); **Joining Words** and/or/but (p.89); **Adverbs of Manner** (p.89); punctuation (p.90). Writing: central idea of the poem; list rhyming pairs and write a poem "Brushing Your Teeth"; Creative Writing: poem from given rhyming pairs (please/lease, turn/learn, find/kind, fuss/us) (p.90).
- **Review 3 (p.91).** -ed words into /d/ /id/ /t/ columns; short vs long vowel word table (p.91); comprehension questions across Units 7–9; alphabetical order (first letter, then second) with dictionary lookups (pp.91–92); meanings (behave, tradition, celebrate, cheerful, invite); modal-verb sentences; circle adverbs in a text; punctuation rewrite; possessive-pronoun cloze; Writing: dialogue about celebrating the spring festival (p.92).
- **Unit 10 — Be Aware, Be Safe (p.93).** Reading: a play-script — Sara, Ahsan and their mother on not taking gifts from strangers (pp.94–95). Sounds: **Weak Forms of 'and', 'of', 'or' and 'but'** (p.96). Speaking: turn-taking dialogue (Teacher/Haris/Jawad); create a dialogue on "Sharing" (p.96). Comprehension: questions; **elements of a story** (Characters, Setting, Beginning, Middle, Conflict, Ending — each defined); September calendar questions; cloze (p.97). Vocabulary: carefully, stranger, friendly, harmful, uncomfortable; **Parts of Speech** sort (Sara, you, permit, nice, mother, harsh, promise…) into Noun/Verbs/Adjectives/Pronouns (p.98). Spelling: missing letters (ch__col__te, c__ndi__s, s__rang__r, pr__mise, s__cret) (p.99). Grammar: **Pronoun-antecedent Agreement** (Read Me + paragraph) (p.99); **Regular Verbs** — base/past/past participle table (start, play, touch, decide) (p.99); **Present, Past and Future Tenses** — three-column sentence table + name-the-tense drill (p.100); **Adverbs of Time** (yesterday, later, tomorrow, now) (p.100); **Full Stop with Abbreviations** (Nov., min., Ltd.; match Aug./St./Dr./Dept.) (pp.100–101). Writing: story web (Character/Setting/Beginning/Conflict/Middle/Solution) → own story; Creative Writing: rules for staying safe (p.101).
- **Unit 11 — The Fox And The Stork (p.102).** Fable in past tense (pp.103–104; the stork's line "Do not ridicule or degrade anyone for any reason" is direct speech inside the story). Sounds: **Intonation Patterns** — rising (end of statements per the book's table; yes-no questions) and falling (final stressed syllable; wh-questions; commands; exclamations) with arrows (pp.104–105). Speaking: three-way dialogue (Stork/Fox/Monkey); agree/disagree politely in groups (p.105). Comprehension: questions; **elements of a fable** (Characters, Setting, Plot, Conflict, Resolution) filled from the story; MCQ cloze (p.106). Vocabulary: clever, teased, beak, degrade, served; **Parts of Speech** from the story (verb/noun/adjective/pronoun/adverb/preposition/conjunction chart); **Alphabetical Order** — dictionary entry anatomy (beak/beam/bean with pronunciation, part of speech, meaning) (p.107). Spelling: missing letters (cu__ning, sto__k, s_rang_r, fr__end, fo__d) (p.108). Grammar: **Subject, Object & Possessive Pronouns** — three-case table with sentence examples (p.108); **Tenses** — The Simple Present Tense, "The Simple Continuous Tense" (present continuous), The Simple Past Tense, each with affirmative/negative/question examples and Read Me definitions (p.109); **Joining Words** and/but/or/because/so (p.109); **Question Words** (Which/What/Where/Why from a word bank) (p.110); **Apostrophe** in contractions (don't, couldn't, they're, I'm) (p.110). Writing: story mind map → own story; Creative Writing: story of two friends reviving friendship after a quarrel (p.110).
- **Unit 12 — Time to Think! (p.111).** Reading: Ahsan, a traffic-rules brochure, and the model formal letter to the Inspector General, Traffic Police Headquarters, Islamabad — with labelled parts (Writer's address, Receiver's address, Date 22 February 2018, Salutation, Body, Signature) (pp.112–114). Sounds: **Weak Forms** of 'and', 'of', 'or', 'but' in sentences (p.114). Speaking: two dialogues (needs, joy, sadness, anger) (p.115). Comprehension: questions (incl. an "interpretive question" Read Me); preposition picture work (in front of, above, between, behind, next to); MCQ cloze (pp.115–117). Vocabulary: citizen, headquarters, traffic police, walker; **spelling change in plural forms** — regular plurals adding 's'/'es'/'ies' (car/cars, bus/buses, story/stories) and irregular plurals (goose/geese, wolf/wolves, man/men, mouse/mice, ox/oxen, tooth/teeth) with the full Read Me rules incl. consonant+y → -ies (pp.117–118). Spelling: read-and-spell word list; missing letters (citi__en, inf__rm, stran__e__). Grammar: **Pointing Words** this/that/these/those (p.118); **The Past Continuous Tense** — explicit structure formulas (Affirmative: Subject + was/were + base form + -ing + object; Negative; Interrogative) + make sentences (p.119); **Connectors** Firstly/Secondly/Finally in a paragraph (p.119); **Hyphens** — uses table (compound numbers twenty-one; join two words good-looking; fractions one-fourth) + hyphenate exercise (p.120). Writing: informal letter to a friend about traffic/safety rules; expository paragraph (model: setting up a computer; write: how to pack your bag); Creative Writing: experience of following traffic rules (p.120).
- **Unit 13 — Little Things (p.121).** Poem "Little Things" by Julia A. Carney (p.123). Oral: **Stress, Rhythm and Intonation** definitions + read a stanza; non-verbal communication signs (celebration, angry, consoling) — guess the emotion game (p.124). Comprehension: questions; map directions with a direction key; MCQ cloze (p.125). Vocabulary: eternity, pleasant, mighty, stray, virtue; **Regular Verb Forms** (allow/behave/jump/talk; write enjoy, kick, need, punish) (p.126). Spelling: circle correct spelling (mighty, heaven, eternity, virtue, pleasant) (p.126). Grammar: **Question Words** — wh- fill-ins + a "How to use" table (When; How many — countable; How much — quantity/price of uncountable) (pp.126–127); **The Simple Future Tense** — structure formulas (Subject + will/shall + base form + object; negative; interrogative) + sentences (p.127); **Kinds of Sentences** — Instructions, Commands, Exclamatory Sentences with Read Me (imperative sentences; instructional sentences) + tick-the-type exercise (p.128); **Questions with 'do' and 'does'** — question/answer table (p.128). Writing: read the model central idea of "Little Things", write the central idea of the third stanza; Creative Writing: poem "My Favourite Thing" from four rhyming pairs (p.129).
- **Review 4 (p.130).** Mark rising/falling intonation; body-language signs; stranger-response discussion (p.130); comprehension (Fox and Stork, traffic rules, respecting others); two words per part of speech (Noun/Verb/Pronoun/Adjective/Adverb/Preposition/Conjunction); meanings (harsh, degrade, jogger, stranger, mighty); write the correct tense of given sentences; rewrite with hyphens; one sentence per sentence kind (Instruction/Command/Exclamatory); connector cloze paragraph; Writing: mind map → story "A brave girl" (p.131).

## 4. Grammar/vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics:** diphthongs (U1, U4) → silent letters k/w/h/l/b + trigraphs tch/sch (U2; silent letters recur U3) → three-consonant clusters initial/final (U5) → sounds of "the" thee/thuh (U6) → long vowel sounds (U7) → short vowel sounds (U8) → three sounds of -ed /d/ /ɪd/ /t/ (U9) → weak forms of and/of/or/but (U10, U12) → intonation patterns rising/falling (U11) → stress, rhythm and intonation + non-verbal communication (U13). The series' most advanced pronunciation book so far: connected-speech and prosody are now explicit.
- **Grammar sequence:** countable/uncountable + collective nouns + adjective types (U1) → verbs, subjects/predicates (U2) → regular/irregular plurals, prepositions of position, capitalisation (U3) → unchanged plurals, helping verbs, prepositions of time, **degrees of adjectives**, capitalisation of titles/events (U4) → gender nouns, be/do/have as helping verbs in negatives, apostrophe for possession (U5) → common/proper nouns, be/do/have as main vs helping verbs (U6) → pronouns + be/do/have with pronouns (U7) → subject/object pronouns, possessive pronouns, can/cannot & may/may not, adverbs (U8) → should/should not, joining words, adverbs of manner, similes (U9) → pronoun-antecedent agreement, regular verb forms, **present/past/future tenses (naming level)**, adverbs of time, abbreviations (U10) → **simple present / present continuous / simple past with affirmative-negative-question patterns**, joining words incl. because/so, question words, apostrophe in contractions (U11) → plural spelling changes, pointing words, **past continuous with structure formulas**, connectors, hyphens (U12) → question words when/how many/how much, **simple future with structure formulas**, sentence kinds, **do/does questions** (U13). Question formation (do/does, was/were inversion) arrives only in the final unit.
- **Vocabulary:** 4–6 glossed words per unit + word-formation strands (antonyms U2, anagrams U3, suffix-based part-of-speech change and synonyms/antonyms U4, homophones U5, compound words U6–U7, adjective strength pairs U8, parts-of-speech sorting U10–U11, dictionary anatomy U11). Dictionary use is by now routine (Reviews 3–4 set dictionary lookups).
- **Writing:** note-taking + mind-mapped sentences → picture/description paragraphs → recipe writing with sequencing connectors → descriptive paragraphs → mind-mapped paragraphs (topic sentence/supporting details/conclusion) → narrative paragraphs → speech-bubble dialogues → invitation + acceptance note → central-idea writing and original poems → story webs and guided stories → formal letter (modelled) + informal letter → expository process paragraphs. The clearest writing progression in the series so far.

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

- **Passive voice:** Absent throughout — no passive structure is named, modelled as a grammar point, or drilled (the medal description "It is composed of 88 percent of copper…" in Unit 4's model paragraph is input only).
- **Conditionals:** Absent as grammar. If-sentences occur once as story input (Unit 10's mother: "If a stranger ever comes close to you…") and are never analysed; no zero/first/second conditional teaching.
- **Reported speech:** Absent. Direct speech is everywhere (dialogues, play-script, quoted speech in stories) but is never converted or taught as reported speech.
- **Tense gaps:** Present perfect and past perfect never appear as taught points (perfect forms occur inside helping-verb examples — "She has bought a new dress", "Tashif's uncle had told us…" — without naming); future continuous/perfect absent; the "future" taught is will/shall + base form only (Unit 13). Irregular verb forms are never tabulated (only regular -ed verbs get base/past/participle tables; irregular plurals are taught but irregular past verbs are not).
- **Also absent:** articles as a system (a/an/the rules from Grades 2–3 are not revisited — "the" appears only as a pronunciation point); comparatives with "more/most" and irregular adjectives (good/better/best); relative clauses; gerunds/infinitives as topics; question tags; modal verbs beyond can/cannot, may/may not, should/should not (no must, would, could, might — could appears only inside the contraction table "could not / couldn't", Unit 11); prepositions beyond position and time sets; there/their/they're distinctions.
- **Exam preparation:** None — Reviews are workbook revision; no test papers, marks schemes or timed practice.
- **Answer keys:** None anywhere in the book.
- **Glossary:** No end glossary (Unit 9's outcomes mention using "textual aids such as the table of contents and glossary", and Unit 11 teaches dictionary anatomy, but the book itself prints no glossary).

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Printed p.43: the Degrees Read Me defines: "Superlative adjectives compare two or more things" — as printed, the superlative is defined with the comparative's scope (two or more).
- Printed p.88: the section is headed "Possessive Pronouns" and its Read Me says "My, our, your, his, her, etc. are possessive pronouns" — the forms listed are possessive adjectives (determiners); the book itself uses "possessive pronouns" correctly for mine/yours/hers in Unit 8 (p.80), so the two units contradict each other.
- Printed p.31: Review 1 asks "Who are the four caliph of Islam?" (singular "caliph" with "four").
- Printed p.104: the intonation table lists rising intonation for "Our voice rises at the end of a statement" with the example "We should follow traffic rules." — statements are conventionally falling; the book's own falling column covers wh-questions and commands, so the statement rule as printed is at odds with standard description (recorded as printed).
- Printed p.109: the present continuous is named "The Simple Continuous Tense" — a non-standard label, as printed.
- Printed p.126: the Regular Verb Forms table sits inside the Vocabulary Building section of Unit 13 (grammar content under the vocabulary heading; the Grammar section proper begins lower on the same page with Question Words).

## 7. Copy defects of this PDF file

- **Watermark:** Every page carries the diagonal "Web version of PCTB Textbook / Not for Sale" watermark. No other stamps; the credits page's "Experimental Edition" box is a printed feature of the book, not a copy defect.
- **Folio continuity:** Printed folios run 1–131 continuously with no missing, duplicated or out-of-order content pages; every unit start matches the contents grid (U1 p.1, U2 p.13, U3 p.22, R1 p.31, U4 p.34, U5 p.45, U6 p.54, R2 p.62, U7 p.65, U8 p.74, U9 p.82, R3 p.91, U10 p.93, U11 p.102, U12 p.111, U13 p.121, R4 p.130). Unlike Grades 1–2, the cover is NOT duplicated at the end of the file: PDF p.138 is the back cover (Urdu national anthem). No blank or truncated pages found.

## Pakistan — English, Grade 5 (file: g05.pdf)

# Pakistan — English Grade 5 — Full Read Draft

## 1. Identity block

- **Title exactly as printed:** The PDF's first page is a plain title page reading "English / 5" over the board's seal — this file contains no illustrated cover and no separate title page beyond it. Unit headings inside call the texts "Lesson 1…13" in the Glossary.
- **Publisher/board as printed:** "Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore" (title page).
- **Curriculum basis as printed:** None printed. Unlike Grades 1–4 in this set, this book carries NO "Based on Single National Curriculum 2020" / "One Nation, One Curriculum" line anywhere on its title or contents pages — it is the board's older-format Grade 5 book (reader-style layout, no Learning Outcomes boxes, no Teaching Point strips).
- **Year / edition / ISBN:** No year, no edition statement and no ISBN printed anywhere in the file. Credits are printed on the contents page (PDF p.2): Authors: Mr. Iftikhar Salim, Mrs. Nabila Gull; Supervised by: Mr. Danish Sarfraz; Layout Setting: Mr. Faizan Waheed; Artist: Aisha Waheed; Editor: Prof. Khalid Mahmood; Reviewed by: Dr. Amra Raza, Ms. Ayesha F. Barque, Mr. Shahzeb Khan; Director Manuscripts: Dr. Mobeen Akhtar; "Illustratrated by: Mr. Umar Khan" (sic — see §6).
- **PDF page count:** 104 PDF pages.
- **Printed-page offset:** Printed page = PDF page − 2. Verified: PDF p.3 = printed 1 (Text 1 opener), PDF p.104 = printed 102 (last Glossary page). PDF p.1 = title page; PDF p.2 = contents + credits. There is no back-cover page in this file — it ends on the Glossary.

## 2. Architecture

A reader-style book, structurally different from the SNC Grades 1–4: **13 numbered texts + a Glossary (printed pp.97–102), and no Reviews**. Each text follows a fixed chain: **Pre-Reading Activity** (2–4 questions) → the reading text (story, biography, exposition or poem, 2–4 pages) → **"How much did you understand?" / Checking Comprehension** (numbered questions, often plus an MCQ or True/False set) → a named **Comprehension Skill** in some texts (Cause and Effect, Predicting Outcome, Main Idea and Details, Fantasy and Realistic Story) → **"Learn about words"** (Vocabulary A: match words to meanings; B: Working with Words — cloze from a word bank) → **Word Study** (a phonics or reference skill) → **Language and Arts** (one grammar topic) → **Writing Skills** (one guided genre task) → **Speaking** (a discussion prompt). Poem texts (4 and 11) add line-completion and rhyming-word work; Text 4 adds a Literary Analysis block. There are no unit Learning Outcomes, no Read Me boxes and no teacher strips.

## 3. Text-by-text (printed page numbers)

- **Text 1 — Hazrat Muhammad's (SAW) Kindness and Forgiveness (p.1).** Reading (pp.1–3): Arabia before Islam; the old woman who threw rubbish and was visited when sick; the enemy with the sword who was forgiven. Comprehension: 5 questions (p.3); **Comprehension Skill: Cause and Effect** — explanation with clue words (so, because, since, that is, why) + complete-the-cause exercise (pp.4–5). Vocabulary: fear, idols, worshipping, ignorance, cruel, practices; cloze bank spreading/confidence/harass/rubbish/reveal (pp.5–6). Phonics: **Silent k, b and w letters** (knot, doubt, write) — find them in sentences (p.6). Language and Arts: **Sentence** (definition: complete sense, capital letter, full stop; mark S/NS) and **Kinds of Sentences — Declarative and Interrogative** (mark D/I) (pp.6–7). Writing: character web (name in the centre oval, five describing words) → paragraph describing the Prophet's (SAW) personality (p.8). Speaking: group discussion — changes Islam brought to society (p.8).
- **Text 2 — The Strange Kettle (p.9).** Folk tale (pp.9–12): the woodcutter, the doubling kettle, the doubled wife and woodcutter, greed repented, kettle returned to the forest. Comprehension: 8 questions (p.12); **Comprehension Skill: Predicting Outcome** as an MCQ set (pp.13–14). Vocabulary: drop, amazed, supper, brass, meal; cloze (pp.14–15). Phonics: **oo Sound in Words** — noon vs look sounds, mark N/L (p.15). Language and Arts: **Imperative and Exclamatory Sentences** (definitions + mark IMP/E) (pp.15–16). Writing: "Writing about a Strange Place" — answer seven prompt questions about a visited place (p.16). Speaking: discuss a folk tale from your own culture (p.16).
- **Text 3 — The Truthful (p.17).** Story (pp.17–18): young Sheikh Abdul Qadir Jillani, the forty gold coins sewn in his shirt, the robbers reformed by his truthfulness. Comprehension: 8 questions (p.18); "Predicting Outcome" heading over an MCQ cloze set (p.19). Vocabulary: saint, caravan, looted, precious, promptly, weep (p.19). Phonics: **Trigraph** — three letters one sound (match, catch, screen, sphere, screem); complete the words (p.20). Language and Arts: **Adverbs of frequency** (never, often, usually, always, sometimes) — definition + fill-ins (p.20). Writing: summary of the story (p.20). Speaking: discuss the story (p.20).
- **Text 4 — Little Things (Poem) (p.21).** Poem by Julia A. Carney (pp.21–22; the same poem that closes Grade 4). Comprehension: main idea in own words, rhyming words, line-completion with options (pp.22–23); **Literary Analysis** — Author's Tone, Author's Purpose, Author's Audience, Author's Point of View (MCQ) (p.23); **Comprehension Skill: Main Idea and Details** — topic sentence/supporting details worked on stanza 1, then applied to the other stanzas (pp.23–24). Vocabulary: grains, humble, beauteous, mighty, ages, eternity, virtue, stray, eden (p.24). Phonics: **Beginning Digraphs** ph and th (photo; this/thing/think) — supply missing digraphs (p.25). Language and Arts: **Noun** (definition + underline) → **Common and Proper Nouns** (table; underline common, circle proper) → **Singular and Plural nouns** with the full spelling-rule set (+s; +es after s/sh/ch/x; consonant+y → ies; vowel+y → +s; f/ef → +s beliefs/roofs, some f/fe → ves wives/wolves) + change-to-plural table (pp.25–27) → **Countable and uncountable nouns** — countable rules incl. a/an with singulars, and uncountables (sugar, water, love, advice…) treated as singular, no indefinite article, partitives "a something of" (a piece of cake, a glass of water), some/any, a little/much; mark C/'un'; choose a/an (pp.28–29). Writing: **Paragraph Outline** — topic sentence + three supporting details on "hardwork", rough draft, revision checklist (title? indent? topic sentence? supporting sentences? order? concluding sentence?), edit, final copy (p.30). Speaking: discuss your mother as role model (p.30).
- **Text 5 — Father of the Nation (p.31).** Biography (pp.31–32): Quaid-e-Azam Muhammad Ali Jinnah — born 25 December 1876 Karachi; Sindh Madrasa-tul-Islam; Lincoln's Inn; law practice; politics from 1906; died 11 September 1948. Comprehension: 5 questions (pp.32–33); "Predicting Outcome" MCQ cloze (p.33). Vocabulary: betterment, misunderstanding, determination, efficient, independence (p.33). Phonics: **Beginning Trigraph** (scrap, schedule, shrink, school, shred) and **Ending Trigraph** (watch, match, sketch, badge, judge) — complete the words (p.34). Writing: six lines on the Quaid from a diagram (place of birth/struggle/education/profession/political career/best quality) (p.34). Speaking: discuss the story in class (p.34). (No Language and Arts section in this text.)
- **Text 6 — A Dream Come True (p.35).** First-person narrative (pp.35–37): a boy holding a balloon rope is lifted to 3000 feet, falls into a pine tree, hospital, scrapbook of press cuttings. Comprehension: 9 questions + "Choose the correct option" MCQ set (pp.37–39). Vocabulary: parachute, stunts, passion, sighted, burners, jerked (p.39). Phonics: **Ending Digraphs** sh, ch, gh, ph (splash, speech, laugh, paragraph) — complete ten words in sentences (p.40). Language and Arts: **Pronouns — Subject and Object pronouns** (definitions and lists; mark SP/OP on underlined pronouns) (pp.40–41). Writing: **Writing a Personal Narrative** — prompt plus a full method page (organise ideas; What/When/Where questions; beginning/middle/ending; rules: good beginning, explain ideas, impressive ending, proofread) (pp.41–42). Speaking: discuss a dream that came true (p.42).
- **Text 7 — The Youngest Genius Arfa Karim (p.43).** Biography (pp.43–44; photo caption "Arfa Karim with Microsoft CEO Bill Gates at Microsoft Headquarters USA"; adapted from "A Brief Biography by Raza Dotana"): youngest MCP at 9 in 2004; Fatimah Jinnah Gold Medal and Salaam Pakistan Youth Award 2005; Pride of Performance; Tech-Ed Barcelona 2006; died 14 January 2012. Comprehension: 5 questions (p.45). Vocabulary: professional, passed away, journey, technology, forums, genius (p.45). Phonics: **Beginning and Ending Consonant Blends** (br, cr, dr; nd, nt) — complete twelve words in sentences (p.46). Language and Arts: **Subject–Verb Agreement** (simple present fill-ins receive/receives…) + **Simple subject and simple predicate** and **Simple Predicate** (underline) (pp.47–48). Writing: **Writing an Informational Article** — collect facts about your city and write from a general sentence (p.48). Speaking: discuss the fact sheet (p.48).
- **Text 8 — Invention of Glass (p.49).** Exposition (pp.49–52): the fishermen legend; "wind's eye" → window; lenses, Galileo 1609, Leeuwenhoek 1675; glass blowing; recycling; safety glass; future glass roads. Comprehension: 10 questions + True/False set (pp.52–53). Vocabulary: substance, puzzled, pressure, extreme, cemented, scarce, afford; cloze bank tough/glow/skyscrapers/asphalt/expensive (pp.53–54). Phonics: **Ending Digraphs th, ng, ck** (bath, bring, back; th's two sounds) — complete the words (p.54). Language and Arts: **Action verb** (a verb shows an action or a state/condition; underline the verb; note items 3 and 9 are the same sentence printed twice) (p.55). Writing: **Learn how to take an Interview** — the four stages (arrangements, preparation, the actual interview, the reconstruction) with detailed guidance, then a model "A student's Interview with a Doctor" with the later exchanges left blank to complete (pp.55–58). Speaking: discuss heroes of your country who made inventions (p.58).
- **Text 9 — Wise Decision (p.59).** Story (pp.59–62): Jan, newly arrived from a village, is ashamed to invite his father to the school hockey match; the father's dignity wins everyone over. Comprehension: 8 questions (p.62) + "Tick the best option" MCQ set (p.63). Vocabulary: sure, refreshments, recently, nearby, handkerchief, laughter, corridor (p.64). Phonics: **Beginning Consonant Blends** qu, squ, str (quack, squeal, straight) — complete the words (pp.64–65). Language and Arts: **Present and Past Tense** — present = happens now, add s/es for 3rd person singular; past spelling rules (drop e + -ed bake–baked; double final consonant hop–hopped; consonant+y → -ied carry–carried); mark verbs PR/PT → **Future Tense** (will + present form; circle the future tense) (pp.65–66). Writing: **Summary Writing** — method (main idea + important details per paragraph); write the summary of paragraph no. 3 (p.66). Speaking: discuss moments you felt proud of your father (p.66).
- **Text 10 — The Invisible Homework (p.67).** Story (pp.67–70): Salim writes homework in lemonade invisible ink, loses and recovers it by the heater, and shows Miss Nadia how the words reappear over heat. Comprehension: 5 questions + MCQ set (pp.70–71). Vocabulary: accept, submit, assignment, disappear, exchange, leaning (p.71). Phonics: **Silent Letters k, w and b** (know, written, thumb) with explanation and circle-the-silent-letter (pp.71–72). Language and Arts: **Adjectives** — an adjective describes a noun or pronoun; answers What kind / How many / Which one; circle the adjective for the underlined noun (p.72). Writing: **Comparing and Contrasting Paragraph** — similarities/differences lists on regular vs invisible ink + concluding sentence (p.73). Speaking: **Debate a Topic** — class split for/against invisible ink (p.73).
- **Text 11 — The Mountain and the Squirrel (Poem) (p.74).** Poem by Ralph Waldo Emerson (pp.74–75): "Talents differ; all is well and wisely put". Comprehension: main idea, lesson, rhyming words; line-completion with options (pp.75–76). Vocabulary: prig, sphere, disgrace, occupy, spry, deny, track; cloze (pp.76–77). Phonics: **Silent Letters gh, h and l** (sighed, oh, walked/daughter) + a **Cloze Story** to complete from a word bank + partner task listing gh/h/l silent words; **Rhyming Words** — underline rhymes per stanza (pp.77–78). Language and Arts: **Adverbs** — describe a verb; tell how/when/where/how often; many end in -ly; circle each adverb and label how/when/where (p.78). (No separate Writing Skills/Speaking block in this text.)
- **Text 12 — The First Straw (p.81).** Story by Nancy DeRoin (pp.81–82): the wise monkey drinks through a reed — "Not the last straw. This is the first!" Comprehension: 9 questions + best-answer MCQ (pp.83–84); **Fantasy and Realistic Story** — definitions + mark story events R/F (p.84). Vocabulary: giant, quiet, reed, safe, strange, die; cloze (p.85). Word Study: **Encyclopedia Skill** — choosing the most likely entry (incl. Wikipedia, www.wikipedia.com as printed) + choose the entry for five enquiries (pp.85–86). Language and Arts: **Punctuation of a Sentence** — capital letter, full stop at end of statement/command, question mark, exclamation mark; rewrite ten unpunctuated lines correctly (pp.86–87). Writing: **Story Structure** — a story has three parts (beginning/middle/end, defined); label story sentences B/M/E; fill a story sheet (Title, Main Characters, Problem, Solution) (pp.87–88). Speaking: "Preservation of Wild Life" discussion — monkeys kept in captivity to dance (p.88).
- **Text 13 — An Ant's Language (p.89).** Exposition by Danielle Martin (pp.89–91): ant colonies; the jam-jar string experiment; scent-trail communication; colony-specific smells. Comprehension: 8 questions (p.91) + problem–solution MCQ analysis (main problem, the first ant's problem, first step, outcome, prediction) (p.92). Vocabulary: trail, colonies, wondered, ceiling, string, disappeared; cloze (p.93). Phonics: **blends spl, spr, thr** (splash, spring, three) — complete and write the whole word; **Compound Words** (candlelight = candle + light) — join words from phrases (bedside, lunchtime, heartbeat…) (p.94). Language and Arts: **Use of Article** — a/an indefinite, the definite; a/an with singular nouns; the with singular or plural; a before consonant sound, an before vowel sound and before silent h (an hour, an honor, an honest man); fill-ins C.1–10 (pp.95–96). Writing: **Description of a place** — write about a park/place where you observed ants (p.96). Speaking: discuss problems caused by ants (p.96).
- **Glossary (pp.97–102).** Per-lesson word lists with glosses for all 13 lessons (Lesson 1 p.97 through Lesson 13 p.102).

## 4. Grammar/vocabulary progression summary

- **Phonics (Word Study):** silent k/b/w (T1) → oo's two sounds (T2) → trigraphs (T3) → beginning digraphs ph/th (T4) → beginning and ending trigraphs (T5) → ending digraphs sh/ch/gh/ph (T6) → blends br/cr/dr/nd/nt (T7) → ending digraphs th/ng/ck (T8) → blends qu/squ/str (T9) → silent k/w/b again (T10) → silent gh/h/l (T11) → encyclopedia/reference skill (T12) → blends spl/spr/thr + compound words (T13). Heavy on digraphs/blends and silent letters; no vowel-system, stress or intonation work at all.
- **Grammar (Language and Arts):** sentence + declarative/interrogative (T1) → imperative/exclamatory (T2) → adverbs of frequency (T3) → nouns: common/proper, full plural spelling rules, countable/uncountable with a/an and partitives (T4 — the book's biggest grammar block) → subject/object pronouns (T6) → subject–verb agreement + simple subject/predicate (T7) → action verbs (T8) → present/past identification with -ed spelling rules + future with will (T9) → adjectives (T10) → adverbs (T11) → sentence punctuation (T12) → articles a/an/the fully (T13). The sequence is a parts-of-speech tour, not a tense system: no verb-form tables, no continuous or perfect aspect anywhere.
- **Reading skills:** a named comprehension skill in several texts (Cause and Effect T1; Predicting Outcome T2/T3/T5 — in practice MCQ cloze; Main Idea and Details + Literary Analysis of tone/purpose/audience/point of view T4; Fantasy vs Realistic T12; problem–solution analysis T13). MCQ comprehension sets are frequent (T2, T5, T6, T9, T10, T12, T13) and True/False appears in T8.
- **Vocabulary:** 5–9 matching words per text + a word-bank cloze, all recycled into the end Glossary (pp.97–102) — the only book in this six-book set with a printed glossary.
- **Writing:** character-web paragraph (T1) → strange-place description (T2) → summary (T3) → outlined paragraph with revision checklist (T4) → diagram-based six lines (T5) → personal narrative with method (T6) → informational article (T7) → interview method + model (T8) → summary writing method (T9) → compare/contrast paragraph (T10) → descriptive paragraph with time-order outline (T11) → story structure sheet (T12) → place description (T13). Speaking is a single discussion/debate prompt per text.

## 5. Honest negatives — what this book never teaches

- **Passive voice:** Never taught. Passive sentences occur inside reading texts (Arfa "was interviewed by all the leading television channels", safety glass "is cemented between two sheets of glass") as input only.
- **Conditionals:** Never taught. One third-conditional sentence appears as a True/False item in Text 8 ("If glass lenses had not been invented, the history of medicine might have been different.") and a first conditional inside Text 10's story — both input only.
- **Reported speech:** Never taught. Direct speech is frequent; one indirect line occurs in Text 3 ("Hazrat Abdul Qadir (RA) told him that it was his mother's advice…") as input only.
- **Tenses:** Only identification-level: present vs past (with -ed spelling rules) and future = will + base form (Text 9). No present/past continuous, no present perfect or any perfect form, no tense tables, no question/negative formation teaching for any tense, no irregular-verb lists (irregular past forms like took/went occur in texts untaught).
- **Also absent:** modal verbs as a topic (can/could/would/should occur in texts only); comparatives/superlatives (poem line "You are not so small as I" is input only); possessive adjectives/pronouns as a topic; prepositions as a topic; relative clauses; conjunctions as a topic (because is a comprehension clue word in T1, not grammar); dictionary skills (an encyclopedia skill instead, T12); letter writing; dialogue writing.
- **Reviews/exam preparation:** None — the book has no review units, no test papers, no marks schemes.
- **Answer keys:** None.
- **Curriculum apparatus:** No Learning Outcomes, no SNC statement, no teacher guidance — see §1.

## 6. Slips / misprints as printed (short fragments, with printed page numbers)

- Contents page (PDF p.2): the credit line reads "Illustratrated by: Mr. Umar Khan" (sic).
- Printed p.27: the plural exercise table header reads "Singlular" (sic).
- Printed p.28: the countable-noun rule lists the words to use with a singular countable noun as "a, the, my, this, with, it" — "with" is not such a word (as printed).
- Printed p.44: the Tech-Ed conference theme is printed as ""Get head of the Game."" (sic).
- Printed p.71–72: the silent-letter explanation reads "in the ending sound for the thumb we hear m sound instead of b" — garbled as printed.
- Printed p.65: an identification exercise item reads "Jan, called his teacher." (comma as printed).
- Printed p.97 (Glossary, Lesson 2): "ashamed — feeling shame and embarrassment about somebody" (as printed).
- Printed p.55: the underline-the-verb exercise prints the sentence "People found many uses of glass." twice (items 3 and 9) — duplicated item as printed.

## 7. Copy defects of this PDF file

- **No watermark or stamp anywhere in this file** (unlike the Grades 1–4 web-version PDFs) — pages are clean.
- **No cover and no back cover in the file:** the PDF opens directly with the plain title page (PDF p.1) and ends with the last Glossary page (PDF p.104 = printed 102). Whether the print original had covers cannot be told from this file.
- **Folio continuity:** Printed folios run 1–102 continuously with no missing, duplicated or out-of-order pages; every text starts exactly where the contents puts it (T1 p.1, T2 p.9, T3 p.17, T4 p.21, T5 p.31, T6 p.35, T7 p.43, T8 p.49, T9 p.59, T10 p.67, T11 p.74, T12 p.81, T13 p.89, Glossary p.97). No blank or truncated pages found. (Claims elsewhere of a folio jump around pp.90–92 do not match this file: folios 89, 90, 91, 92 are all present in order on PDF pp.91–94.)
